Research & Engineering Lineage · Evolved-DRF

Reliability & Maintainability Engineering

Reliability begins with a required function under stated conditions; maintainability asks whether that function can be diagnosed and restored in the real support system. Neither is proved by a healthy component, a mean, a worksheet or a backup.

Source layer: this page retains the original four-family research corpus and its repository-specific v0.4 crosswalk. Its property counts, accepted translations and retained gaps belong to that recorded target scope. The current DRF family record separately identifies corrected scientific subjects, denominators and later receiving. This historical interpretation does not requalify current native implementation.

Genealogy

Reliability and maintainability engineering did not arise as one method. Life-data, survival and renewal theory asked how failures and repairs are distributed for stated populations. Wartime and post-war electronics programmes joined mission requirements, parts, environment, prediction, screening, test and field data. These statistical and institutional lineages supplied conditional evidence, but did not define the required function by themselves.

Failure analysis developed along different paths. Failure-mode and effects analysis worked upwards from ways items can fail; fault trees worked backwards from a defined top event. Reliability growth and failure reporting, analysis and corrective action linked observed failures to design change and recurrence monitoring. Maintainability and supportability became distinct design concerns: diagnosis, access, replaceability, tools, skills, spares, configuration preservation and return-to-service validation contribute to operational downtime, not merely hands-on repair time.

Fault-tolerant computing asked whether a function could continue or recover after activated faults. Redundant logic, voting, recovery blocks, diversity, checkpointing and rollback made coverage and state restoration explicit. Experiments with independently developed software versions later showed that separate teams do not guarantee independent failures. The resulting lesson was not “redundancy never works”, but that common cause, diagnostic coverage, transfer state and spare capacity belong in the claim.

Software reliability added operational input profiles because software does not wear out like a physical component; particular states and inputs activate faults. Dependability taxonomies later related reliability, availability, safety, integrity and maintainability while preserving their differences. Site reliability engineering, service-level objectives, chaos engineering, prognostics and AI-assisted operations are modern translations or hybrids. They add useful mechanisms, but do not form the inevitable final stage of the older disciplines.

Selected sources and claim limits

SourceSupportsDoes not establish
U.S. Advisory Group on Reliability of Electronic Equipment, Reliability of Military Electronic Equipment (1957)A major institutional marker joining requirements, prediction, testing, parts control, data and management.Specific page-level claims from the full report via catalogue metadata, or a universal modern reliability programme.
U.S. Department of Defense, Procedures for Performing a Failure Mode, Effects and Criticality Analysis, and NRC, Fault Tree HandbookSystematic bottom-up failure-mode analysis and deductive top-event analysis.That completing a worksheet controls a failure, or that a tree is complete beyond its chosen event, boundary and dependence assumptions.
Nowlan and Heap, Reliability-Centered MaintenanceMultiple age–reliability patterns and maintenance choices conditioned on failure behaviour and consequence.The same pattern proportions or maintenance policy in every domain.
Avizienis, The N-Version Approach to Fault-Tolerant Software, and Knight and Leveson, An Experimental Evaluation of the Assumption of Independence in Multiversion ProgrammingThe design-diversity proposal and direct evidence that coincident software failures can exceed an independence model.That all diversity is ineffective, or that separate teams create independent failure domains automatically.
Avizienis et al., Basic Concepts and Taxonomy of Dependable and Secure ComputingDistinctions among faults, errors, failures, availability, reliability and maintainability.That a system has achieved any of those attributes merely because it uses the taxonomy.
NIST, Guide for Cybersecurity Event RecoveryRecovery as a people, process and technology path requiring realistic exercises, integrity checks, staging and acceptance criteria.A general reliability result from a cybersecurity guide, or proof from backup creation or service restart alone.
Google, Implementing SLOs, and Nolan, What SRE Could BeUser-relevant objectives as decision aids, plus mechanism-specific criticism of ratios, thresholds, aggregation and adoption.A universal indicator, automatic organisational adoption, causal field proof, or validation of “five nines” without a consumer and denominator.
AWS, Summary of the Amazon S3 Service Disruption in the Northern Virginia Region, and CrowdStrike, Preliminary Post Incident ReviewMechanisms involving operator/tool coupling, shared dependencies, recovery at scale and correlated change.Controlled prevalence or complete independent causal accounts; both are provider-authored postmortems.
Basiri et al., Chaos EngineeringControlled experimentation on a bounded failure hypothesis.General resilience beyond the tested fault, load, configuration, observation period and recovery path.

What survives as engineering practice

The tradition begins by naming the function that matters, who consumes it, the operating mode and environment, the interval or number of demands, acceptable degradation and the consequence of failure. Reliability is avoiding a defined failure over that exposure. Availability concerns whether the function is capable at a point or over time. Maintainability and supportability concern restoration capability and resources. Durability is another claim again. Collapsing them into one “health” number loses the decision.

  • Define complete, partial, intermittent, latent and degraded states only as finely as their consequences require. A binary health bit is sufficient only when the end function is genuinely binary and directly observed.
  • Challenge how faults propagate, where single points and hidden dependencies lie, and which prevention, detection, containment, recovery or accepted-risk control addresses each important path. A focused “what can fail and what happens next?” review is often cheaper than a full worksheet.
  • Credit redundancy only for a declared tolerated fault set with credible independence, detection coverage, switchover, state compatibility and capacity. When independence cannot be supported, treat copies as one failure domain for the decision.
  • Separate detection, diagnosis and recovery. An alarm can be wrong, ambiguous or late; a component can report healthy while the user journey is stale, slow or broken.
  • Allow degraded operation only through a tested contract naming protected and sacrificed functions, consumer impact, observability, duration and exit criteria. Stop safely when continuation has no defensible envelope.
  • Close recovery on authoritative state, dependencies and an end-to-end functional postcondition. Restore sources must be accessible, integral, compatible and exercised under representative conditions.
  • Design maintainability into access, isolation, replacement, configuration/state preservation, validation and support logistics. Replacement or graceful discard may be the better cheap path for low-cost, short-life items.
  • Bind demonstration, field data and inherited evidence to population, operating profile, configuration, assumptions and uncertainty. Material change invalidates only the dependent evidence, which must then be requalified.
  • Call corrective action effective only after the mechanism-targeted fix is deployed and recurrence is assessed against comparable exposure. A closed ticket or falling incident count is not a causal reliability result.

The properties pull against one another. Redundancy can tolerate independent faults while adding control, maintenance and common-cause paths. Fast failover can reduce interruption while transferring stale state or overload to a weak standby. Preventive maintenance can reduce age-related failures or induce new ones when the mechanism is not age-related. Rich diagnosis can improve isolation while creating false actions and operator burden. Higher availability can conflict with forensic preservation or safe stopping after an unknown corruption.

How the tradition changed, and what v0.4 translates

The mature field moved from component means towards mission and user-journey semantics; from assumed independence towards common-cause evidence; from prevention alone towards detection, containment, bounded degradation, repair and validated recovery; from universal scheduled maintenance towards mechanism- and consequence-based policy; and from handbook predictions towards calibrated test and field evidence. Service objectives and chaos experiments survive only as scoped decision instruments. Predictive maintenance survives only where a precursor is observable, uncertainty is calibrated, lead time is actionable and simpler policies are compared.

IMPLEMENTAUDIT translates only the parts that fit audit-governed repository work. Its accepted v0.4 architecture preserves atomic configuration/package identity, dependency-correct parallel work, one writer at joins, nested loops for action and recurrence, and a separation between model judgement and deterministic authority. Those mechanisms can support configuration freshness, correlated-dependency reasoning, corrective-action closure and bounded automation.

They do not amount to a general reliability-and-maintainability contract. The research family would require explicit mission states, common-cause analysis, diagnostic coverage, degradation criteria and restoration behaviour before those claims could be made. The recorded original v0.4 mapping intentionally retains those gaps rather than treating architectural similarity as closure.

What was already native

IMPLEMENTAUDIT already retained failures and contrary evidence, required owner and source, recorded rollback and reopen conditions, stopped on abnormality, bound packages and releases to exact identities, and verified post-action state. These controls supply useful failure, currentness and recovery ingredients. Its recurrence loop also asks whether a repair addressed the mechanism instead of merely suppressing one symptom.

Native overlap remains bounded. A rollback plan is not tested disaster recovery; a deterministic package check is not a field-reliability demonstration; repeated green runs do not establish independence or a failure probability; and post-action readback does not by itself measure maintainability or service durability.

Rejected caricatures, universals and ceremonies

The lineage rejects mean time between failures as the universal meaning of reliability; “five nines” without an indicator, consumer, denominator, window and exclusions; the bathtub curve as a law for every population; handbook prediction as field truth; FMEA/FMECA completion as proof of controlled modes; redundancy diagrams as independence evidence; green component health as end-to-end availability; backup existence as recovery; one failover or chaos exercise as general resilience; incident counts or fitted growth curves as causal improvement; and zero observed failures as zero failure probability.

No reliability score, uptime target, mandatory worksheet, redundancy quota, permanent maintenance programme, predictive-maintenance requirement or AI auto-remediation mode was imported. Metrics and artefacts may answer a scoped question, but their presence cannot substitute for required-function evidence.

Evidence and generalisation limits

The frozen corpus examined 55 properties across 85 exact source records. Mathematical models are conditional on failure processes, distributions and dependence assumptions. Tests are conditional on sample, profile, duration and configuration. Incident reports establish mechanisms, not prevalence. Service and chaos guidance records practices and obligations, not automatic effectiveness. Current prognostics and AI/autonomy evidence remains use-case-specific, with transfer, drift, false action and authority still open.

The accepted crosswalk is especially conservative here. Required-function semantics and end-to-end journey checks retain deterministic fixture gaps; failure-mode analysis, failure-state semantics, common-cause reasoning, diagnostic coverage and maintainability retain source-level proof gaps; redundancy, graceful degradation and recovery retain behavioural proof gaps. Many other properties remain bounded with no new proof claim. This route therefore reports no measured reliability, availability, maintainability, supportability, diagnostic coverage, recovery time, service durability or probability of failure. Verification of selected v0.4 architecture relations is not evidence that those domain obligations are implemented or effective.

Provenance

This synthesis uses the frozen 12 August 2026 Reliability and Maintainability Engineering corpus: its 55-property ledger, 85-record source table, external report, audit intake, public-documentation intake and integrity manifests. Direct transmission, historical inference, convergent engineering and domain translation are kept distinct; analogy is not promoted into ancestry or effectiveness. ERM-P001 — Explicit required-function and mission semantics
PROPERTY_ID

ERM-P001

PROPERTY_NAME

Explicit required-function and mission semantics

HISTORICAL_ORIGIN

Wartime/post-war equipment reliability requirements; mission-success language in military reliability programmes; later dependability standards.

LINEAGE_CLASS

  1. HARDWARE_RELIABILITY_LINEAGE

  2. CONVERGENT_ENGINEERING

RELATIONSHIP_TO_OTHER_TRADITIONS

  1. RELIABILITY_ENGINEERING_NATIVE

  2. SYSTEMS_ENGINEERING_IMPORT_OR_SHARED_ANCESTRY

  3. CONVERGENT_PROPERTY

ORIGINAL_FORM

State a numerical reliability objective for an item over a mission time.

PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED

A probability has no engineering meaning until the required function, consumer, conditions, interval and failure threshold are fixed.

REQUIRED_FUNCTION_OR_MISSION

The named system or service function must meet specified performance, timing, state and consequence criteria for the declared consumer.

FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT

Failure is non-performance, untimely performance, erroneous performance or performance below the declared threshold—not merely component death.

MECHANISM

Define function, mission/service, modes, performance thresholds, failure scoring, consumer and mission time/demand before modelling or testing.

TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT

Any reliability, availability, demonstration, redundancy, recovery or service-level claim that could drive design, release, maintenance or acceptance.

NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH

For low-consequence reversible work, use a direct pass/fail functional check and a short observation window rather than a probabilistic programme.

DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS

Stakeholder/consumer criteria, mission phases, state authority, measurable outputs and an agreed scoring rule.

OPERATIONAL_PROFILE_PRECONDITIONS

A declared and sufficiently representative operating profile, environment, duty cycle, mission phase and maintenance state.

FAILURE_MODEL_PRECONDITIONS

A failure model appropriate to the estimand; distinguish non-repairable life, recurrent repairable events, demand failures, software activation and service outcomes.

REDUNDANCY_OR_COMMON_CAUSE_PRECONDITIONS

No redundancy premise is needed; if redundancy appears in the implementation, common-cause and switchover assumptions must be analysed separately.

DIAGNOSTIC_COVERAGE_PRECONDITIONS

Observable functional outcomes sufficient to determine whether the claimed property actually holds.

RECOVERY_OR_REPAIR_PRECONDITIONS

No recovery premise is inherent; any claim that depends on restoration must invoke explicit recovery postconditions.

MAINTAINABILITY_PRECONDITIONS

The design and support system must not make verification or corrective action infeasible.

EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF

Prevents metric optimisation for a component or proxy while the actual mission/user function fails.

DECISION_OR_CONSUMER

Mission owner, service consumer, operator, maintainer, acceptance authority or risk decision-maker.

KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES

Ambiguous verbs; moving thresholds; component-centric scoring; omitted partial failures; disputed responsibility boundaries.

IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS

Requirements can be bureaucratic or falsely precise; rare severe consequences can be hidden by an average success definition.

HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED

Component survival targets were broadened to mission reliability, functional failure, user journeys and consequence-aware degraded states.

MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM

A testable, versioned mission contract that binds required function, mode, profile, environment, interval/demand, degraded acceptance and consumer consequence.

CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY

A reliability requirement template is optional; the property is semantic closure on what success and failure mean.

ERM-P002 — Operational-profile, environment and demand definition
PROPERTY_ID

ERM-P002

PROPERTY_NAME

Operational-profile, environment and demand definition

HISTORICAL_ORIGIN

Life testing and field-data stratification; software operational-profile work in the early 1990s; modern service and mission profiling.

LINEAGE_CLASS

  1. LIFE_DATA_AND_STATISTICAL_RELIABILITY_LINEAGE

  2. SOFTWARE_RELIABILITY_LINEAGE

  3. HYBRID

RELATIONSHIP_TO_OTHER_TRADITIONS

  1. STATISTICAL_RELIABILITY_ANCESTRY

  2. RELIABILITY_ENGINEERING_NATIVE

  3. DOMAIN_TRANSLATION

ORIGINAL_FORM

Estimate reliability under a specified duty cycle and environment or sample test operations in field-frequency proportion.

PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED

Failure probability changes with usage, load, environment, mode, maintenance, software inputs and dependencies.

REQUIRED_FUNCTION_OR_MISSION

The function in ERM-P001 under the actual or deliberately bounded distribution of demands and conditions.

FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT

Evidence or design appears reliable under a convenient profile but fails under real frequency, severity, mode transition or rare consequence-important demand.

MECHANISM

Version and measure workloads, mission phases, physical stresses, user classes, duty cycles, dependencies, maintenance states and rare critical operations.

TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT

Prediction, demonstration, accelerated testing, software testing, SLO definition, capacity planning, maintenance policy or field-transfer claim.

NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH

Use a small set of bounding scenarios when a full probability distribution would not change the decision.

DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS

Telemetry or credible mission analysis, stable definitions, sampling weights and explicit treatment of rare high-consequence demands.

OPERATIONAL_PROFILE_PRECONDITIONS

A declared and sufficiently representative operating profile, environment, duty cycle, mission phase and maintenance state.

FAILURE_MODEL_PRECONDITIONS

A failure model appropriate to the estimand; distinguish non-repairable life, recurrent repairable events, demand failures, software activation and service outcomes.

REDUNDANCY_OR_COMMON_CAUSE_PRECONDITIONS

No redundancy premise is needed; if redundancy appears in the implementation, common-cause and switchover assumptions must be analysed separately.

DIAGNOSTIC_COVERAGE_PRECONDITIONS

Observable functional outcomes sufficient to determine whether the claimed property actually holds.

RECOVERY_OR_REPAIR_PRECONDITIONS

No recovery premise is inherent; any claim that depends on restoration must invoke explicit recovery postconditions.

MAINTAINABILITY_PRECONDITIONS

The design and support system must not make verification or corrective action infeasible.

EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF

Reduces false confidence from laboratory, average-load or dominant-user evidence that excludes the conditions causing failure.

DECISION_OR_CONSUMER

Test planner, reliability analyst, service owner, maintainer, release authority and mission decision-maker.

KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES

Stale profiles; survivorship bias; request-weighting that hides users; omitted maintenance/change states; extrapolation beyond observed support.

IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS

Profiles can encode current behaviour and suppress novel uses; high-consequence rare events may deserve more weight than frequency implies.

HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED

Frequency-only profiles evolved toward stratified, consequence-aware and continuously refreshed exposure models.

MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM

A configuration-bound, uncertainty-labelled exposure model that supports both representative sampling and explicit stress/boundary cases.

CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY

A workload spreadsheet is optional; the property is evidence about where, how often and under what conditions function is demanded.

CLAIM

A configuration-bound, uncertainty-labelled exposure model that supports both representative sampling and explicit stress/boundary cases.

POPULATION_OR_DENOMINATOR

Declared units, missions, demands, requests, users, time windows or recurrent-event process.

CONFIGURATION_IDENTITY

Exact hardware, software, data, model, dependency and procedure versions.

OPERATIONAL_PROFILE

Representative demand/workload/environment with consequence-important rare cases identified.

Predeclared sampling/censoring, failure scoring, exposure and stopping rules.

MODEL

Model selected for the stated estimand: life distribution, recurrent-event process, binomial demand, availability state model or direct functional check.

Intervals, posterior uncertainty or bounded qualitative confidence; point estimates alone are insufficient.

CENSORING_AND_MISSINGNESS

Right/interval censoring, unreported events, lost telemetry and excluded windows are recorded.

COVERAGE

Claim coverage is limited to tested/observed failure modes, environments and paths.

Test planner, reliability analyst, service owner, maintainer, release authority and mission decision-maker.

CHEAP_PATH

Use a small set of bounding scenarios when a full probability distribution would not change the decision.

CONTRARY_EVIDENCE

No fixed profile remains valid after material product, dependency, user or mission change.

EXPIRY_OR_FRESHNESS

Evidence expires when configuration, profile, environment, support organisation or dependencies materially change.

MATURE_FORM

A configuration-bound, uncertainty-labelled exposure model that supports both representative sampling and explicit stress/boundary cases.

DISPOSITION_RATIONALE

Admitted or rejected according to mechanism, trigger, assumption validity, evidence and anti-ceremony analysis.

ERM-P003 — Explicit complete, partial, intermittent, latent and degraded failure states
PROPERTY_ID

ERM-P003

PROPERTY_NAME

Explicit complete, partial, intermittent, latent and degraded failure states

HISTORICAL_ORIGIN

FMEA/FMECA failure-mode taxonomies, multi-state reliability, maintainability and dependable-computing fault/error/failure distinctions.

LINEAGE_CLASS

  1. FAILURE_ANALYSIS_LINEAGE

  2. AVAILABILITY_AND_REPAIRABLE_SYSTEMS_LINEAGE

  3. CONVERGENT_ENGINEERING

RELATIONSHIP_TO_OTHER_TRADITIONS

  1. RELIABILITY_ENGINEERING_NATIVE

  2. FAULT_TOLERANCE_IMPORT_OR_SHARED_ANCESTRY

  3. CONVERGENT_PROPERTY

ORIGINAL_FORM

Classify each item as operating or failed and enumerate failure modes.

PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED

Binary up/down states erase partial service, false output, intermittency, dormant standby defects and acceptable degradation.

REQUIRED_FUNCTION_OR_MISSION

Required function and quality thresholds for each operating mode, including which reduced capabilities remain acceptable and for how long.

FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT

A harmful state is classified as “up,” or an acceptable degraded state is counted as complete failure, corrupting decisions and metrics.

MECHANISM

Define state space, observable criteria, transitions, dwell limits, latent conditions, false indications and consumer consequences.

TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT

Systems with multiple modes, redundancy, standby, partial service, performance thresholds, intermittent faults or hidden protective functions.

NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH

A binary state is sufficient only when the consumer consequence is genuinely binary and the observation tests the complete function.

DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS

State observability, mission criteria, transition rules, timing and authority for accepting/ending degradation.

OPERATIONAL_PROFILE_PRECONDITIONS

A declared and sufficiently representative operating profile, environment, duty cycle, mission phase and maintenance state.

FAILURE_MODEL_PRECONDITIONS

A failure model appropriate to the estimand; distinguish non-repairable life, recurrent repairable events, demand failures, software activation and service outcomes.

REDUNDANCY_OR_COMMON_CAUSE_PRECONDITIONS

No redundancy premise is needed; if redundancy appears in the implementation, common-cause and switchover assumptions must be analysed separately.

DIAGNOSTIC_COVERAGE_PRECONDITIONS

Observable functional outcomes sufficient to determine whether the claimed property actually holds.

RECOVERY_OR_REPAIR_PRECONDITIONS

No recovery premise is inherent; any claim that depends on restoration must invoke explicit recovery postconditions.

MAINTAINABILITY_PRECONDITIONS

The design and support system must not make verification or corrective action infeasible.

EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF

Enables meaningful availability, diagnostics, degradation and recovery claims instead of uptime theatre.

DECISION_OR_CONSUMER

Operator, mission owner, service consumer, model builder and incident reviewer.

KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES

State explosion; inconsistent scoring; unobservable latent states; “degraded” used to normalise unacceptable service.

IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS

Multi-state models can create false precision and obscure simpler decisive thresholds.

HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED

Binary life models were supplemented by functional failure, multi-state availability, degraded modes and user-impact measures.

MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM

A minimal consequence-relevant state model with explicit observation, entry/exit criteria, allowed duration and treatment of latent states.

CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY

A state diagram is optional; the property is unambiguous classification of the states that matter to the mission.

Explicit complete, partial, intermittent, latent and degraded failure states

REQUIRED_FUNCTION

MISSION_OR_SERVICE

Required function and quality thresholds for each operating mode, including which reduced capabilities remain acceptable and for how long.

OPERATING_PROFILE

Declared demands, workload mix, duty cycle, mode changes and maintenance states.

ENVIRONMENT

Declared physical, software, organisational and dependency environment.

FAILURE_DEFINITION

A harmful state is classified as “up,” or an acceptable degraded state is counted as complete failure, corrupting decisions and metrics.

DEGRADED_ACCEPTABLE_STATE

NEXT_LEVEL_EFFECT

END_EFFECT

A harmful state is classified as “up,” or an acceptable degraded state is counted as complete failure, corrupting decisions and metrics.

ERM-P004 — System boundary and failure-attribution discipline
PROPERTY_ID

ERM-P004

PROPERTY_NAME

System boundary and failure-attribution discipline

HISTORICAL_ORIGIN

Fault-tree ground rules, RBD success paths, field failure reporting and service dependency analysis.

LINEAGE_CLASS

  1. FAILURE_ANALYSIS_LINEAGE

  2. SERVICE_RELIABILITY_OR_SRE_TRANSLATION

  3. CONVERGENT_ENGINEERING

RELATIONSHIP_TO_OTHER_TRADITIONS

  1. RELIABILITY_ENGINEERING_NATIVE

  2. SYSTEMS_ENGINEERING_IMPORT_OR_SHARED_ANCESTRY

  3. CONVERGENT_PROPERTY

ORIGINAL_FORM

Choose the item under analysis and attribute each observed failure to a component.

PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED

Changing the item boundary or excluding dependencies, maintenance, planned windows or user-side effects can manufacture reliability.

REQUIRED_FUNCTION_OR_MISSION

The end-to-end function across all components, services, people and support resources inside the declared responsibility/decision boundary.

FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT

A mission failure is omitted, double-counted or assigned to an external party so that no owner sees the actual system risk.

MECHANISM

Declare analysis boundary, inclusions/exclusions, interfaces, responsibility versus causal attribution, denominator and disputed cases.

TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT

RBD/FTA/FMEA, SLA/SLO, supplier reliability, incident metrics, field-return analysis or cross-organisation service paths.

NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH

For a local reversible decision, state a narrow boundary explicitly and avoid claiming end-to-end reliability.

DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS

Dependency inventory, interface contracts, event correlation, shared clocks/identifiers and adjudication rules.

OPERATIONAL_PROFILE_PRECONDITIONS

A declared and sufficiently representative operating profile, environment, duty cycle, mission phase and maintenance state.

FAILURE_MODEL_PRECONDITIONS

A failure model appropriate to the estimand; distinguish non-repairable life, recurrent repairable events, demand failures, software activation and service outcomes.

REDUNDANCY_OR_COMMON_CAUSE_PRECONDITIONS

No redundancy premise is needed; if redundancy appears in the implementation, common-cause and switchover assumptions must be analysed separately.

DIAGNOSTIC_COVERAGE_PRECONDITIONS

Observable functional outcomes sufficient to determine whether the claimed property actually holds.

RECOVERY_OR_REPAIR_PRECONDITIONS

No recovery premise is inherent; any claim that depends on restoration must invoke explicit recovery postconditions.

MAINTAINABILITY_PRECONDITIONS

The design and support system must not make verification or corrective action infeasible.

EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF

Prevents local optimisation and denominator gaming while preserving causal learning across interfaces.

DECISION_OR_CONSUMER

System owner, supplier manager, SRE/service owner, maintainer and acceptance authority.

KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES

Boundary disputes; “external cause” exclusion; planned-maintenance exclusions; missing user telemetry; ownership confused with causation.

IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS

Very broad boundaries make measurement and accountability difficult; too-narrow boundaries make claims irrelevant.

HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED

Component attribution evolved toward end-to-end dependency and consumer-consequence accounting while retaining local causal analysis.

MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM

A layered boundary model: end-to-end consequence for reliability claims, plus component/dependency attribution for corrective action.

CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY

A service map or fault tree is optional; the property is stable scope and transparent exclusions.

ERM-P005 — Separate reliability, availability, maintainability, supportability and durability claims
PROPERTY_ID

ERM-P005

PROPERTY_NAME

Separate reliability, availability, maintainability, supportability and durability claims

HISTORICAL_ORIGIN

Post-war reliability/maintainability programmes, repairable-system probability, logistics support and current IEC dependability vocabulary.

LINEAGE_CLASS

  1. HARDWARE_RELIABILITY_LINEAGE

  2. MAINTAINABILITY_LINEAGE

  3. AVAILABILITY_AND_REPAIRABLE_SYSTEMS_LINEAGE

  4. CONVERGENT_ENGINEERING

RELATIONSHIP_TO_OTHER_TRADITIONS

  1. RELIABILITY_ENGINEERING_NATIVE

  2. MAINTAINABILITY_NATIVE

  3. HYBRID_RESOLUTION

ORIGINAL_FORM

Report MTBF, MTTR and availability as related but distinct quantities.

PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED

High availability can result from frequent fast repairs; high reliability can coexist with slow repair; maintainable design can fail operationally for lack of support.

REQUIRED_FUNCTION_OR_MISSION

A declared mission interval and restoration/support conditions for the item or service.

FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT

A decision substitutes one RAM measure for another and accepts the wrong failure frequency, downtime, repair burden or life limitation.

MECHANISM

Use distinct estimands and state models: no-failure probability, probability of being up, restoration-time distribution, support delay and life/degradation measures.

TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT

Any claim using reliability, uptime, availability, MTBF, MTTR, serviceability, durability or operational readiness.

NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH

Use direct failure count and downtime decomposition when a full stochastic model is unnecessary; never collapse the labels.

DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS

Consistent clocks, failure/restoration definitions, exposure, repair-completion criteria and support-delay data.

OPERATIONAL_PROFILE_PRECONDITIONS

A declared and sufficiently representative operating profile, environment, duty cycle, mission phase and maintenance state.

FAILURE_MODEL_PRECONDITIONS

A failure model appropriate to the estimand; distinguish non-repairable life, recurrent repairable events, demand failures, software activation and service outcomes.

REDUNDANCY_OR_COMMON_CAUSE_PRECONDITIONS

No redundancy premise is needed; if redundancy appears in the implementation, common-cause and switchover assumptions must be analysed separately.

DIAGNOSTIC_COVERAGE_PRECONDITIONS

Observable functional outcomes sufficient to determine whether the claimed property actually holds.

RECOVERY_OR_REPAIR_PRECONDITIONS

No recovery premise is inherent; any claim that depends on restoration must invoke explicit recovery postconditions.

MAINTAINABILITY_PRECONDITIONS

Access, modularity, safe isolation, procedures, test equipment, spares, tools, skills, logistics, configuration data and time under actual field conditions.

EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF

Makes design and logistics trade-offs visible and blocks “fast repair” from masquerading as “few failures.”

DECISION_OR_CONSUMER

Mission planner, operator, maintenance/logistics owner, procurement and service consumer.

KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES

Mean measures hide tails; repair clock starts/stops inconsistently; excluded maintenance windows; inherent availability presented as operational availability.

IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS

The vocabulary can become metric-heavy; definitions still need mission consequence and state validity.

HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED

Simple component MTBF expanded into mission reliability, inherent/achieved/operational availability, maintainability and supportability.

MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM

A small RAM measure set tied to explicit functions, state transitions, total downtime components and decision thresholds.

CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY

A RAM dashboard is optional; the property is semantic and denominator integrity across distinct attributes.

RELIABILITY_EVIDENCE_PROFILE
APPLICABLE

PROPERTY

Separate reliability, availability, maintainability, supportability and durability claims

CLAIM

A small RAM measure set tied to explicit functions, state transitions, total downtime components and decision thresholds.

POPULATION_OR_DENOMINATOR

Declared units, missions, demands, requests, users, time windows or recurrent-event process.

CONFIGURATION_IDENTITY

Exact hardware, software, data, model, dependency and procedure versions.

OPERATIONAL_PROFILE

MODEL

CENSORING_AND_MISSINGNESS

COVERAGE

ERM-P006 — Systematic failure-mode, effect and propagation challenge
PROPERTY_ID

ERM-P006

PROPERTY_NAME

Systematic failure-mode, effect and propagation challenge

HISTORICAL_ORIGIN

1949 military FMECA, later MIL-STD-1629A, FTA and current IEC 60812.

LINEAGE_CLASS

  1. FAILURE_ANALYSIS_LINEAGE

  2. HYBRID

RELATIONSHIP_TO_OTHER_TRADITIONS

  1. RELIABILITY_ENGINEERING_NATIVE

  2. SAFETY_IMPORT_OR_SHARED_ANCESTRY

  3. CONVERGENT_PROPERTY

ORIGINAL_FORM

Enumerate component failure modes and document local, next-higher and end effects in a worksheet.

PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED

Nominal architecture descriptions do not reveal plausible loss, erroneous output, timing, interface or propagation paths.

REQUIRED_FUNCTION_OR_MISSION

The function and boundary defined by ERM-P001–P004.

FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT

A plausible mode or propagation path remains uncontrolled because analysis stopped at a component label or single failure.

MECHANISM

Challenge functions and interfaces with plausible modes; trace local-to-end effects, controls, detectability, combinations and evidence for treatment.

TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT

Novel/high-consequence functions, complex interfaces, weak field history, design change, repeated incidents or uncertain propagation.

NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH

Use a focused “what can fail and what happens next?” review for simple reversible systems; do not require a full worksheet.

DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS

Current architecture/configuration, knowledgeable cross-functional participants, explicit ground rules and closure evidence.

OPERATIONAL_PROFILE_PRECONDITIONS

A declared and sufficiently representative operating profile, environment, duty cycle, mission phase and maintenance state.

FAILURE_MODEL_PRECONDITIONS

A failure model appropriate to the estimand; distinguish non-repairable life, recurrent repairable events, demand failures, software activation and service outcomes.

REDUNDANCY_OR_COMMON_CAUSE_PRECONDITIONS

No redundancy premise is needed; if redundancy appears in the implementation, common-cause and switchover assumptions must be analysed separately.

DIAGNOSTIC_COVERAGE_PRECONDITIONS

Observable functional outcomes sufficient to determine whether the claimed property actually holds.

RECOVERY_OR_REPAIR_PRECONDITIONS

No recovery premise is inherent; any claim that depends on restoration must invoke explicit recovery postconditions.

MAINTAINABILITY_PRECONDITIONS

The design and support system must not make verification or corrective action infeasible.

EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF

Finds single points, latent paths, missing diagnostics and consequence escalation early enough to change design or test.

DECISION_OR_CONSUMER

Designer, reliability engineer, maintainer, tester, operator and acceptance authority.

KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES

Checklist copying; unbounded rows; subjective ranks; omission of software/human/dependent combinations; controls listed but unverified.

IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS

Bottom-up FMEA can miss interactions and multiple failures; FTA can inherit an incomplete top event and independence assumptions.

HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED

Worksheet-centric analysis evolved toward functional, interface, dynamic, dependency and evidence-linked analysis chosen by question.

MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM

A proportionate, configuration-bound adversarial analysis that links each important failure path to prevention, detection, containment, recovery or accepted residual risk.

CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY

The FMEA/FMECA table is not the property; systematic challenge and verified closure are.

STRONGLY_RETAINED

HIGH

HIGH

  1. SRC-053

  2. SRC-058

  3. SRC-059

  4. SRC-060

EMPIRICAL_OR_FIELD_EVIDENCE

Major outages repeatedly involve coupled operations, dependencies and recovery paths that a part-only checklist would miss (SRC-058–SRC-062).

CONTRARY_EVIDENCE

No method is complete for unknown failure modes or all combinations; analysis must be tested and refreshed.

OPEN_QUESTIONS

How can dynamic/software interactions be covered without producing unreviewable model size?

FAILURE_PROPAGATION_PROFILE
APPLICABLE

PROPERTY

Systematic failure-mode, effect and propagation challenge

INITIATING_FAULT_OR_FAILURE

A plausible mode or propagation path remains uncontrolled because analysis stopped at a component label or single failure.

LOCAL_EFFECT

Loss, corruption, delay, false indication or degraded performance at the initiating item.

NEXT_LEVEL_EFFECT

Effect on the enclosing function, dependency path or maintenance process.

END_EFFECT

A plausible mode or propagation path remains uncontrolled because analysis stopped at a component label or single failure.

PROPAGATION_PATH

Functional, physical, state, timing, control, resource or organisational coupling.

CONTAINMENT_BOUNDARY

A declared fault-containment region or explicit stop/degradation boundary.

DEPENDENCIES

Current architecture/configuration, knowledgeable cross-functional participants, explicit ground rules and closure evidence.

COMMON_CAUSE_OR_SHARED_COUPLING

Shared specification, environment, supplier, control plane, state, maintenance or operator path must be challenged.

LATENT_CONDITION

Standby, monitoring, repair or dependency defects may remain latent until demand.

DETECTION

Outcome- or function-relevant observation, not a component heartbeat alone.

CONTROL

Challenge functions and interfaces with plausible modes; trace local-to-end effects, controls, detectability, combinations and evidence for treatment.

RESIDUAL_RISK

Unmodelled combinations, dependence, coverage gaps and changed configuration remain.

REDUNDANCY_COMMON_CAUSE_PROFILE
APPLICABLE

false

PROFILE_TYPE

REDUNDANCY_COMMON_CAUSE_PROFILE

The property does not itself claim redundancy benefit; any redundant implementation must invoke ERM-P008/ERM-P009.

false

ERM-P007 — Single-point and latent-failure exposure control
PROPERTY_ID

ERM-P007

PROPERTY_NAME

Single-point and latent-failure exposure control

HISTORICAL_ORIGIN

FMECA critical-item lists, fault trees, standby-system analysis and built-in test.

LINEAGE_CLASS

  1. FAILURE_ANALYSIS_LINEAGE

  2. FAULT_TOLERANT_COMPUTING_LINEAGE

  3. CONVERGENT_ENGINEERING

RELATIONSHIP_TO_OTHER_TRADITIONS

  1. RELIABILITY_ENGINEERING_NATIVE

  2. FAULT_TOLERANCE_IMPORT_OR_SHARED_ANCESTRY

  3. CONVERGENT_PROPERTY

ORIGINAL_FORM

Identify any single item whose failure causes mission loss and any dormant protective item whose failure is hidden.

PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED

A nominally protected system can have a hidden one-shot failure at a switch, sensor, shared utility, standby channel or maintenance state.

REQUIRED_FUNCTION_OR_MISSION

Specified fault tolerance or degraded mission capability under the declared initiating fault set.

FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT

One fault, or one fault plus an undetected latent condition, defeats required function.

MECHANISM

Identify cut sets and latent protective failures; eliminate, isolate, monitor or periodically proof-test them; bound exposure time.

TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT

Redundancy, standby, protective functions, emergency equipment, rarely demanded recovery paths and maintenance bypasses.

NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH

For simple systems, trace the end-to-end path once and directly exercise the spare/protective function rather than build a full quantitative tree.

DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS

Complete boundary, realistic initial state, demand rate, proof-test interval, detection coverage and restoration plan.

OPERATIONAL_PROFILE_PRECONDITIONS

A declared and sufficiently representative operating profile, environment, duty cycle, mission phase and maintenance state.

FAILURE_MODEL_PRECONDITIONS

A failure model appropriate to the estimand; distinguish non-repairable life, recurrent repairable events, demand failures, software activation and service outcomes.

REDUNDANCY_OR_COMMON_CAUSE_PRECONDITIONS

Credible failure-domain independence, common-cause analysis, standby condition, switchover coverage and state compatibility.

DIAGNOSTIC_COVERAGE_PRECONDITIONS

Enumerated fault/failure population, observable signals, calibrated thresholds, isolation resolution, false-alarm and missed-detection costs.

RECOVERY_OR_REPAIR_PRECONDITIONS

No recovery premise is inherent; any claim that depends on restoration must invoke explicit recovery postconditions.

MAINTAINABILITY_PRECONDITIONS

The design and support system must not make verification or corrective action infeasible.

EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF

Prevents false redundancy and limits time spent unknowingly one fault away from mission loss.

DECISION_OR_CONSUMER

Architect, maintainer, operator, tester and mission-risk authority.

KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES

Hidden bypasses; common sensor/switch; proof test that does not test the whole path; maintenance disables both channels; dormant ageing.

IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS

“Single failure” focus can distract from correlated multi-fault and systemic conditions.

HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED

Static single-point lists evolved to minimal cut sets, latent-exposure intervals, dynamic standby and common-cause analysis.

MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM

Explicit minimal failure sets including latent states, with elimination or tested detection/repair intervals matched to consequence.

CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY

A critical-items list is optional; the property is actual removal or bounded exposure of decisive failure sets.

CURRENT_STATUS

STRONGLY_RETAINED

  2. SRC-005

  3. SRC-028

  4. SRC-053

CRITICAL_SOURCES

  1. SRC-052

  2. SRC-054

  3. SRC-060

EMPIRICAL_OR_FIELD_EVIDENCE

GitLab’s failed backup/replication paths and Meta’s simultaneous loss of normal and out-of-band access demonstrate latent and shared recovery-path exposure (SRC-059, SRC-060).

CONTRARY_EVIDENCE

A zero single-point-failure count does not prove resilience to dependent combinations.

OPEN_QUESTIONS

How should latent exposure be prioritised when proof testing itself creates maintenance risk?

APPLICABLE

true

PROPERTY

Single-point and latent-failure exposure control

REDUNDANCY_FORM

Active, standby, voting, diverse, replicated or alternate-path redundancy as explicitly declared.

CLAIMED_TOLERANCE

The specified number/class of faults tolerated while meeting required function or an accepted degraded state.

FAILURE_DOMAINS

Physical, power, network, control-plane, state, software, supplier, operator and maintenance domains.

MATURE_FORM

DIAGNOSTIC_COVERAGE_PROFILE
APPLICABLE

true

PROPERTY

Single-point and latent-failure exposure control

TARGET_FAULTS_FAILURES

One fault, or one fault plus an undetected latent condition, defeats required function.

OBSERVABLES

Function outputs, timing, state, residuals, dependency signals and user/mission outcomes.

DETECTION_COVERAGE

Coverage is stated for an enumerated fault/failure population, operating mode and latency window.

FALSE_ALARM_COST

Unnecessary maintenance, failover, shutdown, operator load or customer disruption.

MISSED_DETECTION_COST

Continued propagation, latent damage, missed recovery window or false assurance.

ISOLATION_RESOLUTION

Replaceable unit, functional region, dependency or ambiguity group appropriate to the action.

AMBIGUITY_GROUPS

Plausible causes producing the same symptom are preserved until discriminated.

LATENCY

Detection and isolation time are bounded relative to propagation and recovery deadlines.

CONFIDENCE_CALIBRATION

Uncertainty, thresholds, drift and out-of-distribution conditions are exposed.

OPERATOR_OR_AUTOMATION_ACTION

Action is authority-bounded, reversible where feasible and matched to diagnosis confidence.

RECOVERY_COVERAGE

A detected fault counts only if the prescribed action restores or contains the target failure with known coverage.

MATURE_FORM

Explicit minimal failure sets including latent states, with elimination or tested detection/repair intervals matched to consequence.

ERM-P008 — Common-cause, common-mode and correlated-dependency analysis
PROPERTY_ID

ERM-P008

PROPERTY_NAME

Common-cause, common-mode and correlated-dependency analysis

HISTORICAL_ORIGIN

Nuclear and aerospace common-cause analysis, diversity debates, multiversion software experiments and cloud dependency failures.

LINEAGE_CLASS

  1. FAULT_TOLERANT_COMPUTING_LINEAGE

  2. FAILURE_ANALYSIS_LINEAGE

  3. SERVICE_RELIABILITY_OR_SRE_TRANSLATION

  4. HYBRID

RELATIONSHIP_TO_OTHER_TRADITIONS

  1. RELIABILITY_ENGINEERING_NATIVE

  2. FAULT_TOLERANCE_IMPORT_OR_SHARED_ANCESTRY

  3. DOMAIN_TRANSLATION

  4. HYBRID_RESOLUTION

ORIGINAL_FORM

Apply a beta factor or common-cause event category to redundant components.

PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED

Nominal parallel paths share specification, design, environment, power, control, state, supplier, maintenance, deployment or operator causes.

REQUIRED_FUNCTION_OR_MISSION

Continued or degraded required function after the claimed fault set.

FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT

Several “independent” elements fail together or sequentially from a shared coupling, invalidating the parallel reliability calculation.

MECHANISM

Enumerate failure domains and coupling factors; seek empirical CCF data; model dependence; separate shared controls; test correlated scenarios; reduce concentration.

TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT

Any redundancy, multi-region, multi-vendor, diverse implementation, backup, standby or third-party dependency claim.

NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH

When independence cannot be supported, treat copies as one failure domain for the decision and avoid precision theatre.

DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS

Architecture/dependency visibility, configuration/supplier data, incident linkage and credible dependence parameters or conservative bounds.

OPERATIONAL_PROFILE_PRECONDITIONS

A declared and sufficiently representative operating profile, environment, duty cycle, mission phase and maintenance state.

FAILURE_MODEL_PRECONDITIONS

A failure model appropriate to the estimand; distinguish non-repairable life, recurrent repairable events, demand failures, software activation and service outcomes.

REDUNDANCY_OR_COMMON_CAUSE_PRECONDITIONS

Credible failure-domain independence, common-cause analysis, standby condition, switchover coverage and state compatibility.

DIAGNOSTIC_COVERAGE_PRECONDITIONS

Observable functional outcomes sufficient to determine whether the claimed property actually holds.

RECOVERY_OR_REPAIR_PRECONDITIONS

No recovery premise is inherent; any claim that depends on restoration must invoke explicit recovery postconditions.

MAINTAINABILITY_PRECONDITIONS

The design and support system must not make verification or corrective action infeasible.

EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF

Avoids orders-of-magnitude overstatement of redundancy benefit and exposes hidden ecosystem concentration.

DECISION_OR_CONSUMER

Architect, reliability modeller, service owner, procurement/supplier manager and mission-risk authority.

KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES

Unobserved coupling; common specification; shared update; shared operator; correlated maintenance; regional/control-plane dependence; sparse CCF data.

IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS

CCF parameters are uncertain and domain-specific; pessimistic bounding can erase economically useful redundancy.

HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED

Assumed independence evolved to explicit failure-domain, beta/alpha-factor, diversity and correlated-dependency treatment.

MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM

A qualitative-plus-quantitative coupling argument that states what is shared, what is separated, the evidence for residual dependence and consequences if the assumption fails.

CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY

A “multi-AZ” label or beta-factor entry is not the property; credible domain separation and dependence evidence are.

CURRENT_STATUS

REDUNDANCY_COMMON_CAUSE_PROPERTY

CONTRARY_EVIDENCE_STRENGTH

PRIMARY_SOURCES

  1. SRC-014

  2. SRC-015

  3. SRC-052

  4. SRC-053

  5. SRC-055

CRITICAL_SOURCES

  1. SRC-054

  2. SRC-058

  3. SRC-059

  4. SRC-061

  5. SRC-062

  6. SRC-063

EMPIRICAL_OR_FIELD_EVIDENCE

Meta’s backbone/DNS/tool coupling, AWS’s S3 dependency fan-out and CrowdStrike’s homogeneous update propagation provide direct incident support (SRC-058, SRC-059, SRC-061, SRC-062).

CONTRARY_EVIDENCE

Redundancy still pays when failure domains and transfer paths are credibly separated; the criticism is of assumed independence, not redundancy itself.

OPEN_QUESTIONS

How can dependence be bounded in rapidly changing service ecosystems with sparse joint-failure observations?

ERM-P009 — Redundancy only with credible independence, coverage and switchover
PROPERTY_ID

ERM-P009

PROPERTY_NAME

Redundancy only with credible independence, coverage and switchover

HISTORICAL_ORIGIN

Voting and standby reliability, von Neumann’s unreliable-component constructions, aerospace fault tolerance and high-availability failover.

LINEAGE_CLASS

  1. HARDWARE_RELIABILITY_LINEAGE

  2. FAULT_TOLERANT_COMPUTING_LINEAGE

  3. AVAILABILITY_AND_REPAIRABLE_SYSTEMS_LINEAGE

  4. HYBRID

RELATIONSHIP_TO_OTHER_TRADITIONS

  1. RELIABILITY_ENGINEERING_NATIVE

  2. FAULT_TOLERANCE_IMPORT_OR_SHARED_ANCESTRY

  3. HYBRID_RESOLUTION

ORIGINAL_FORM

Place independent parallel components in an RBD or vote among replicas to multiply down failure probability.

PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED

Copy count is mistaken for fault tolerance while detection, standby health, transfer logic, state and common causes remain unproven.

REQUIRED_FUNCTION_OR_MISSION

Full or declared degraded mission function after the fault class and within the transfer deadline.

FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT

Both paths fail together, the spare is latent-bad, switchover does not occur, transfer corrupts state, capacity is insufficient or adjudication selects the wrong output.

MECHANISM

Specify tolerated faults; isolate domains; monitor standby; validate detection/decision/transfer/state/capacity; test under representative load and failed-control conditions.

TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT

N+1/N+2, TMR, replicas, active-active, hot/warm/cold standby, alternate path, multi-region or recovery-block claim.

NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH

Prefer one simple, observable system or a manual spare when added redundancy creates more coupling than risk reduction.

DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS

ERM-P008 common-cause analysis, diagnostic coverage, switchover reliability, compatible state, spare capacity and maintainable configuration.

OPERATIONAL_PROFILE_PRECONDITIONS

A declared and sufficiently representative operating profile, environment, duty cycle, mission phase and maintenance state.

FAILURE_MODEL_PRECONDITIONS

A failure model appropriate to the estimand; distinguish non-repairable life, recurrent repairable events, demand failures, software activation and service outcomes.

REDUNDANCY_OR_COMMON_CAUSE_PRECONDITIONS

Credible failure-domain independence, common-cause analysis, standby condition, switchover coverage and state compatibility.

DIAGNOSTIC_COVERAGE_PRECONDITIONS

Observable functional outcomes sufficient to determine whether the claimed property actually holds.

RECOVERY_OR_REPAIR_PRECONDITIONS

A valid recovery source, authorised action, state/data reconciliation, dependency readiness and independently checked return-to-service postconditions.

MAINTAINABILITY_PRECONDITIONS

The design and support system must not make verification or corrective action infeasible.

EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF

Provides continuity or bounded degradation for credible fault classes without converting diagrams into false assurance.

DECISION_OR_CONSUMER

Architect, operator, mission owner and acceptance authority.

KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES

Voter/switch single point; dormant spare; split brain; stale state; overload after failover; simultaneous maintenance; common update/specification.

IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS

Redundancy adds components, interfaces, operational modes and maintenance burden; diversity can impair maintainability.

HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED

Simple parallel blocks evolved to coverage-aware, state-aware and failure-domain-aware fault tolerance.

MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM

A fault-tolerance claim stated as tolerated fault set × coverage × independence × transfer/state/capacity evidence, with residual common-cause risk.

CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY

A redundancy diagram is optional; the property is an evidenced alternate path that actually assumes the mission.

CURRENT_STATUS

REDUNDANCY_COMMON_CAUSE_PROPERTY

EVIDENCE_STRENGTH
HISTORICAL_PROVENANCE_STRENGTH

HIGH

FORMAL_OR_PROBABILISTIC_STRENGTH

VERY_HIGH

LIFE_DATA_OR_FIELD_STRENGTH

HIGH

DEMONSTRATION_TEST_STRENGTH

HIGH

HIGH

OPERATIONAL_PRACTICE_STRENGTH

HIGH

REPLICATION_OR_MULTI_STUDY_STRENGTH

LOW_TO_MODERATE

TRANSFERABILITY_STRENGTH

HIGH

ASSUMPTION_SENSITIVITY

VERY_HIGH

CONTRARY_EVIDENCE_STRENGTH

HIGH

PRIMARY_SOURCES

  1. SRC-011

  2. SRC-012

  3. SRC-013

  4. SRC-023

  5. SRC-028

CRITICAL_SOURCES

  1. SRC-014

  2. SRC-015

  3. SRC-052

  4. SRC-053

  5. SRC-054

  6. SRC-055

EMPIRICAL_OR_FIELD_EVIDENCE

GitLab’s nominal standby and backup paths were not usable as assumed; AWS recovery showed restart scale and capacity safeguards mattered (SRC-058, SRC-060).

CONTRARY_EVIDENCE

Under validated independence and coverage, mathematical and field evidence strongly support redundancy for specified faults.

OPEN_QUESTIONS

What minimum evidence justifies numerical independence rather than a conservative correlated model?

DETECTION

Outcome- or function-relevant observation, not a component heartbeat alone.

CONTROL

Specify tolerated faults; isolate domains; monitor standby; validate detection/decision/transfer/state/capacity; test under representative load and failed-control conditions.

RESIDUAL_RISK

Unmodelled combinations, dependence, coverage gaps and changed configuration remain.

REDUNDANCY_COMMON_CAUSE_PROFILE
APPLICABLE

true

PROPERTY

Redundancy only with credible independence, coverage and switchover

REDUNDANCY_FORM

Active, standby, voting, diverse, replicated or alternate-path redundancy as explicitly declared.

CLAIMED_TOLERANCE

The specified number/class of faults tolerated while meeting required function or an accepted degraded state.

FAILURE_DOMAINS

Physical, power, network, control-plane, state, software, supplier, operator and maintenance domains.

INDEPENDENCE_EVIDENCE

Evidence must address shared causes rather than infer independence from copy count or labels.

COMMON_CAUSE_COUPLINGS

Specification, design, implementation, environment, supplier, maintenance, configuration and operational coupling.

STANDBY_LATENCY_OR_DORMANCY

Dormant faults, activation delay and cold/warm/hot readiness are measured when applicable.

SWITCHOVER_COVERAGE

Detection, decision, transfer, state synchronisation and post-transfer functional success.

STATE_CONSISTENCY

The takeover state and authority must be current, complete and semantically valid.

DEGRADED_MODE

Declared reduced capability and exit conditions.

TEST_OR_FIELD_EVIDENCE

Representative failover/failure tests plus field or incident evidence where available.

MATURE_FORM

A fault-tolerance claim stated as tolerated fault set × coverage × independence × transfer/state/capacity evidence, with residual common-cause risk.

PROPERTY

Redundancy only with credible independence, coverage and switchover

INITIATING_FAILURE

Both paths fail together, the spare is latent-bad, switchover does not occur, transfer corrupts state, capacity is insufficient or adjudication selects the wrong output.

RECOVERY_ACTION

Specify tolerated faults; isolate domains; monitor standby; validate detection/decision/transfer/state/capacity; test under representative load and failed-control conditions.

AUTHORITY_SOURCE

The designated current configuration/state authority and authorised operator or controller.

CHECKPOINT_BACKUP_SPARE_OR_ALTERNATE

RPO_OR_STATE_LOSS_BOUND

Explicit allowed state/data loss or “none” for state-continuous functions.

RTO_OR_RESTORATION_BOUND

STATE_RECONCILIATION

FUNCTIONAL_POSTCONDITIONS

Full or declared degraded mission function after the fault class and within the transfer deadline.

DATA_STATE_POSTCONDITIONS

DEPENDENCY_POSTCONDITIONS

VALIDATION

ROLLBACK_OR_FALLBACK

MATURE_FORM

ERM-P010 — Fault containment regions and propagation barriers
PROPERTY_ID

ERM-P010

PROPERTY_NAME

Fault containment regions and propagation barriers

HISTORICAL_ORIGIN

Hardware isolation and protection, recovery blocks, fault-containment regions, partitioning/cells and blast-radius engineering.

LINEAGE_CLASS

  1. FAULT_TOLERANT_COMPUTING_LINEAGE

  2. FAILURE_ANALYSIS_LINEAGE

  3. SERVICE_RELIABILITY_OR_SRE_TRANSLATION

  4. HYBRID

RELATIONSHIP_TO_OTHER_TRADITIONS

  1. FAULT_TOLERANCE_IMPORT_OR_SHARED_ANCESTRY

  2. RELIABILITY_ENGINEERING_NATIVE

  3. DOMAIN_TRANSLATION

ORIGINAL_FORM

Physically or logically isolate faults so they cannot cross a defined region.

PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED

A local defect, overload, bad update or repair action propagates into a system-wide outage or corrupts recovery sources.

REQUIRED_FUNCTION_OR_MISSION

Critical function remains available, degrades within criteria or can be restored without contamination beyond the region.

FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT

Shared state, control, resource pools, retries, maintenance or deployment bypass the intended boundary.

MECHANISM

Partition state/resources/control; enforce quotas and circuit boundaries; stage deployment; isolate maintenance; define escape paths; test boundary breach.

TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT

High fan-out dependencies, untrusted/rapid changes, fault tolerance, multi-tenant resources, recovery sources and high-consequence functions.

NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH

Use simple rate limits, manual change gates or one-way isolation when full cell architecture is disproportionate.

DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS

Known propagation mechanisms, independent controls, observability at boundaries and recovery capacity outside the failed region.

OPERATIONAL_PROFILE_PRECONDITIONS

A declared and sufficiently representative operating profile, environment, duty cycle, mission phase and maintenance state.

FAILURE_MODEL_PRECONDITIONS

A failure model appropriate to the estimand; distinguish non-repairable life, recurrent repairable events, demand failures, software activation and service outcomes.

REDUNDANCY_OR_COMMON_CAUSE_PRECONDITIONS

Credible failure-domain independence, common-cause analysis, standby condition, switchover coverage and state compatibility.

DIAGNOSTIC_COVERAGE_PRECONDITIONS

Observable functional outcomes sufficient to determine whether the claimed property actually holds.

RECOVERY_OR_REPAIR_PRECONDITIONS

A valid recovery source, authorised action, state/data reconciliation, dependency readiness and independently checked return-to-service postconditions.

MAINTAINABILITY_PRECONDITIONS

The design and support system must not make verification or corrective action infeasible.

EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF

Limits blast radius, preserves diagnostic evidence and leaves unaffected capacity for recovery.

DECISION_OR_CONSUMER

Architect, operator, incident commander, maintainer and mission owner.

KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES

Control plane shared by all cells; global configuration; retry storms; corrupt replicated state; operator/tool privileges cross boundaries.

IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS

Partitioning increases cost, consistency and operational complexity; false isolation can hide accumulating latent faults.

HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED

Physical isolation broadened to software cells, staged rollout, resource isolation, state quarantine and organisational blast-radius controls.

MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM

A tested containment argument covering state, control, resources, dependencies, maintenance and recovery—not merely data-plane topology.

CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY

A cell diagram or “bulkhead” label is optional; the property is measured containment of specified propagation.

Containment cannot eliminate shared specification, governance or supply-chain common causes by itself.

ERM-P011 — Fault detection coverage tied to required function
PROPERTY_ID

ERM-P011

PROPERTY_NAME

Fault detection coverage tied to required function

HISTORICAL_ORIGIN

Built-in test, fault coverage in fault-tolerant systems, model-based residuals and service monitoring.

LINEAGE_CLASS

  1. DIAGNOSTICS_AND_PHM_LINEAGE

  2. FAULT_TOLERANT_COMPUTING_LINEAGE

  3. SERVICE_RELIABILITY_OR_SRE_TRANSLATION

  4. HYBRID

RELATIONSHIP_TO_OTHER_TRADITIONS

  1. MAINTAINABILITY_NATIVE

  2. FAULT_TOLERANCE_IMPORT_OR_SHARED_ANCESTRY

  3. DOMAIN_TRANSLATION

ORIGINAL_FORM

Assign a coverage probability that a component fault is detected and correctly handled.

PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED

Nominal reliability or redundancy assumes faults are noticed before propagation or before a standby demand, but monitoring sees only a subset.

REQUIRED_FUNCTION_OR_MISSION

Timely recognition that the required function or a protective/recovery path has entered a defined failed/degraded state.

FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT

A green component signal coexists with broken end-to-end function, or the target fault is outside the monitor’s coverage.

MECHANISM

Define the fault/failure population; map each to observables and latency; measure misses/false alarms; include end-to-end functional probes and dormant paths.

TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT

Automatic failover, latent protective functions, remote operation, SLO alerting, PHM or any claim using “coverage.”

NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH

A direct manual functional test may dominate elaborate monitoring for infrequent, low-cost, reversible decisions.

DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS

Observable consequences, representative fault injection/field labels, threshold calibration, independent monitoring dependencies and action path.

OPERATIONAL_PROFILE_PRECONDITIONS

A declared and sufficiently representative operating profile, environment, duty cycle, mission phase and maintenance state.

FAILURE_MODEL_PRECONDITIONS

A failure model appropriate to the estimand; distinguish non-repairable life, recurrent repairable events, demand failures, software activation and service outcomes.

REDUNDANCY_OR_COMMON_CAUSE_PRECONDITIONS

No redundancy premise is needed; if redundancy appears in the implementation, common-cause and switchover assumptions must be analysed separately.

DIAGNOSTIC_COVERAGE_PRECONDITIONS

Enumerated fault/failure population, observable signals, calibrated thresholds, isolation resolution, false-alarm and missed-detection costs.

RECOVERY_OR_REPAIR_PRECONDITIONS

No recovery premise is inherent; any claim that depends on restoration must invoke explicit recovery postconditions.

MAINTAINABILITY_PRECONDITIONS

The design and support system must not make verification or corrective action infeasible.

EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF

Turns “health” from a heartbeat into evidence that target failures are likely to be noticed in time.

DECISION_OR_CONSUMER

Operator, maintainer, automated recovery controller and mission owner.

KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES

Silent semantic failure; monitor shares failed dependency; stale telemetry; test-mode-only coverage; false green after partial recovery.

IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS

Coverage numbers are often generated from an incomplete fault list and can be falsely precise.

HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED

One-bit BIT and liveness evolved toward function-level, multi-signal and user-journey monitoring with explicit coverage gaps.

MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM

A coverage claim indexed by fault class, operating mode, latency and end-to-end consequence, validated independently of the monitored component.

CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY

A health dashboard is optional; the property is known detection performance over the relevant failure population.

CURRENT_STATUS

Meta’s DNS servers were operational but unreachable; internal diagnostic tools were also lost, showing local health and diagnostic independence can both fail (SRC-059).

CONTRARY_EVIDENCE

No monitor can prove absence of unknown latent failures; claims must remain coverage-bounded.

How can semantic/user-journey coverage be measured when ground truth is delayed or disputed?

FALSE_ALARM_COST

ISOLATION_RESOLUTION

LATENCY

ERM-P012 — Diagnostic isolation, ambiguity and confidence discipline
PROPERTY_ID

ERM-P012

PROPERTY_NAME

Diagnostic isolation, ambiguity and confidence discipline

HISTORICAL_ORIGIN

Troubleshooting/testability, model-based diagnosis, ambiguity groups and PHM.

LINEAGE_CLASS

  1. DIAGNOSTICS_AND_PHM_LINEAGE

  2. MAINTAINABILITY_LINEAGE

  3. HYBRID

RELATIONSHIP_TO_OTHER_TRADITIONS

  1. MAINTAINABILITY_NATIVE

  2. RELIABILITY_ENGINEERING_NATIVE

  3. HYBRID_RESOLUTION

ORIGINAL_FORM

Localise a detected fault to a line-replaceable unit so maintenance can replace it.

PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED

An alarm identifies a symptom but not the actionable cause, leading to wrong replacement, unsafe automation or long downtime.

REQUIRED_FUNCTION_OR_MISSION

Identify a cause set at the resolution needed for safe containment, repair or recovery within the mission deadline.

FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT

Premature root-cause certainty, unresolved ambiguity, confounded dependency symptoms or diagnosis drift triggers ineffective action.

MECHANISM

Maintain hypotheses/ambiguity groups; seek discriminating observations; calibrate confidence; match action reversibility and authority to uncertainty.

TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT

Multiple plausible causes, costly/irreversible repair, automatic remediation, dependency failures or repeated “no fault found.”

NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH

When several causes share the same safe reversible remedy, act on the symptom and defer exact root cause.

DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS

Causal/functional model, sensor validity, fault labels, access to dependency state, time budget and action-cost model.

OPERATIONAL_PROFILE_PRECONDITIONS

A declared and sufficiently representative operating profile, environment, duty cycle, mission phase and maintenance state.

FAILURE_MODEL_PRECONDITIONS

A failure model appropriate to the estimand; distinguish non-repairable life, recurrent repairable events, demand failures, software activation and service outcomes.

REDUNDANCY_OR_COMMON_CAUSE_PRECONDITIONS

No redundancy premise is needed; if redundancy appears in the implementation, common-cause and switchover assumptions must be analysed separately.

DIAGNOSTIC_COVERAGE_PRECONDITIONS

Enumerated fault/failure population, observable signals, calibrated thresholds, isolation resolution, false-alarm and missed-detection costs.

RECOVERY_OR_REPAIR_PRECONDITIONS

A valid recovery source, authorised action, state/data reconciliation, dependency readiness and independently checked return-to-service postconditions.

MAINTAINABILITY_PRECONDITIONS

Access, modularity, safe isolation, procedures, test equipment, spares, tools, skills, logistics, configuration data and time under actual field conditions.

EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF

Reduces parts-cannon maintenance, repeated restarts and harmful automation while improving restoration time.

DECISION_OR_CONSUMER

Maintainer, operator, automated controller, incident commander and design owner.

KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES

Sensor fault mistaken for process fault; correlated symptoms; training/domain shift; confirmation bias; unavailable evidence during outage.

IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS

Root-cause analysis can become post-hoc narrative; exact isolation may cost more time than safe restoration permits.

HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED

Deterministic fault codes evolved toward probabilistic/model-based diagnosis with explicit ambiguity, confidence and decision coupling.

MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM

Decision-sufficient diagnosis: calibrated cause set, evidence, ambiguity, latency and reversible action—not forced single-cause certainty.

CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY

A root-cause label is not the property; discriminating evidence and action-relevant confidence are.

CURRENT_STATUS

DIAGNOSTIC_COVERAGE_PROPERTY

EVIDENCE_STRENGTH
HISTORICAL_PROVENANCE_STRENGTH

HIGH

FORMAL_OR_PROBABILISTIC_STRENGTH

HIGH

LIFE_DATA_OR_FIELD_STRENGTH

MODERATE

MODERATE

HIGH

OPERATIONAL_PRACTICE_STRENGTH

HIGH

REPLICATION_OR_MULTI_STUDY_STRENGTH

LOW_TO_MODERATE

TRANSFERABILITY_STRENGTH

MODERATE_TO_HIGH

ASSUMPTION_SENSITIVITY

HIGH

CONTRARY_EVIDENCE_STRENGTH

MODERATE

PRIMARY_SOURCES

  1. SRC-009

  2. SRC-024

  3. SRC-025

  4. SRC-026

  5. SRC-027

CRITICAL_SOURCES

  1. SRC-056

  2. SRC-059

  3. SRC-072

  4. SRC-076

EMPIRICAL_OR_FIELD_EVIDENCE

Large outages show diagnosis can be slowed because the same dependency failure removes normal tools or access (SRC-059).

CONTRARY_EVIDENCE

For simple deterministic faults, direct tests can establish cause cheaply; probabilistic diagnosis should not be mandatory.

OPEN_QUESTIONS

How should diagnosis confidence be calibrated for rare faults and changing configurations with sparse labels?

Premature root-cause certainty, unresolved ambiguity, confounded dependency symptoms or diagnosis drift triggers ineffective action.

PROPAGATION_PATH

Functional, physical, state, timing, control, resource or organisational coupling.

CONTAINMENT_BOUNDARY

A declared fault-containment region or explicit stop/degradation boundary.

DEPENDENCIES

Causal/functional model, sensor validity, fault labels, access to dependency state, time budget and action-cost model.

COMMON_CAUSE_OR_SHARED_COUPLING

Shared specification, environment, supplier, control plane, state, maintenance or operator path must be challenged.

LATENT_CONDITION

Standby, monitoring, repair or dependency defects may remain latent until demand.

DETECTION

Outcome- or function-relevant observation, not a component heartbeat alone.

CONTROL

Maintain hypotheses/ambiguity groups; seek discriminating observations; calibrate confidence; match action reversibility and authority to uncertainty.

RESIDUAL_RISK

Unmodelled combinations, dependence, coverage gaps and changed configuration remain.

REDUNDANCY_COMMON_CAUSE_PROFILE
APPLICABLE

false

PROFILE_TYPE

REDUNDANCY_COMMON_CAUSE_PROFILE

REASON

The property does not itself claim redundancy benefit; any redundant implementation must invoke ERM-P008/ERM-P009.

DIAGNOSTIC_COVERAGE_PROFILE
APPLICABLE

true

PROPERTY

Diagnostic isolation, ambiguity and confidence discipline

TARGET_FAULTS_FAILURES

Premature root-cause certainty, unresolved ambiguity, confounded dependency symptoms or diagnosis drift triggers ineffective action.

OBSERVABLES

Function outputs, timing, state, residuals, dependency signals and user/mission outcomes.

DETECTION_COVERAGE

Coverage is stated for an enumerated fault/failure population, operating mode and latency window.

FALSE_ALARM_COST

Unnecessary maintenance, failover, shutdown, operator load or customer disruption.

MISSED_DETECTION_COST

Continued propagation, latent damage, missed recovery window or false assurance.

ISOLATION_RESOLUTION

Replaceable unit, functional region, dependency or ambiguity group appropriate to the action.

AMBIGUITY_GROUPS

Plausible causes producing the same symptom are preserved until discriminated.

LATENCY

Detection and isolation time are bounded relative to propagation and recovery deadlines.

CONFIDENCE_CALIBRATION

Uncertainty, thresholds, drift and out-of-distribution conditions are exposed.

OPERATOR_OR_AUTOMATION_ACTION

Action is authority-bounded, reversible where feasible and matched to diagnosis confidence.

RECOVERY_COVERAGE

A detected fault counts only if the prescribed action restores or contains the target failure with known coverage.

MATURE_FORM

Decision-sufficient diagnosis: calibrated cause set, evidence, ambiguity, latency and reversible action—not forced single-cause certainty.

RECOVERY_RESTORATION_PROFILE
APPLICABLE

true

PROPERTY

Diagnostic isolation, ambiguity and confidence discipline

INITIATING_FAILURE

Premature root-cause certainty, unresolved ambiguity, confounded dependency symptoms or diagnosis drift triggers ineffective action.

RECOVERY_ACTION

Maintain hypotheses/ambiguity groups; seek discriminating observations; calibrate confidence; match action reversibility and authority to uncertainty.

AUTHORITY_SOURCE

The designated current configuration/state authority and authorised operator or controller.

CHECKPOINT_BACKUP_SPARE_OR_ALTERNATE

Declared checkpoint, replica, backup, spare, rebuild source or manual workaround.

RPO_OR_STATE_LOSS_BOUND

Explicit allowed state/data loss or “none” for state-continuous functions.

RTO_OR_RESTORATION_BOUND

Explicit time-to-restoration bound including diagnosis, logistics, repair and validation.

STATE_RECONCILIATION

Resolve divergent, stale, partial or replayed state before authoritative service resumes.

FUNCTIONAL_POSTCONDITIONS

Identify a cause set at the resolution needed for safe containment, repair or recovery within the mission deadline.

DATA_STATE_POSTCONDITIONS

Integrity, completeness, freshness, ordering and authority appropriate to the mission.

DEPENDENCY_POSTCONDITIONS

Critical dependencies and capacity are verified rather than assumed from local restart.

VALIDATION

Independent end-to-end functional and state checks under representative demand.

RETURN_TO_SERVICE_AUTHORITY

Named human or automated authority with explicit acceptance criteria.

ROLLBACK_OR_FALLBACK

A bounded fallback when restoration validation fails or the original cause persists.

MATURE_FORM

Decision-sufficient diagnosis: calibrated cause set, evidence, ambiguity, latency and reversible action—not forced single-cause certainty.
