ERM-P013 — Explicit false-positive, false-negative and action cost
PROPERTY_ID

ERM-P013

PROPERTY_NAME

Explicit false-positive, false-negative and action cost

HISTORICAL_ORIGIN

Statistical detection theory, BIT false alarms, condition monitoring and modern alerting/anomaly detection.

LINEAGE_CLASS

  1. DIAGNOSTICS_AND_PHM_LINEAGE

  2. LIFE_DATA_AND_STATISTICAL_RELIABILITY_LINEAGE

  3. SERVICE_RELIABILITY_OR_SRE_TRANSLATION

  4. HYBRID

RELATIONSHIP_TO_OTHER_TRADITIONS

  1. STATISTICAL_RELIABILITY_ANCESTRY

  2. MAINTAINABILITY_NATIVE

  3. DOMAIN_TRANSLATION

ORIGINAL_FORM

Specify detection probability and false-alarm rate for a built-in test.

PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED

Increasing sensitivity can create maintenance-induced failures, alert fatigue or needless shutdown, while low sensitivity misses the recovery window.

REQUIRED_FUNCTION_OR_MISSION

A monitoring/diagnostic decision that minimises mission-relevant expected loss under declared consequences and constraints.

FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT

A threshold is selected from model accuracy alone without the cost of unnecessary action, delay, missed failure or operator saturation.

MECHANISM

Estimate conditional errors by mode/profile; attach action/consequence costs; calibrate thresholds; monitor drift; permit abstention/manual review.

TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT

Anomaly detection, PHM, health checks, alerting, automatic failover/remediation or costly inspection/replacement.

NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH

Use a conservative rule or human confirmation when event rate is low and labels cannot support fine calibration.

DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS

Ground truth or adjudicated outcomes, base rates, representative profile, action-cost model and feedback after action.

OPERATIONAL_PROFILE_PRECONDITIONS

A declared and sufficiently representative operating profile, environment, duty cycle, mission phase and maintenance state.

FAILURE_MODEL_PRECONDITIONS

A failure model appropriate to the estimand; distinguish non-repairable life, recurrent repairable events, demand failures, software activation and service outcomes.

REDUNDANCY_OR_COMMON_CAUSE_PRECONDITIONS

No redundancy premise is needed; if redundancy appears in the implementation, common-cause and switchover assumptions must be analysed separately.

DIAGNOSTIC_COVERAGE_PRECONDITIONS

Enumerated fault/failure population, observable signals, calibrated thresholds, isolation resolution, false-alarm and missed-detection costs.

RECOVERY_OR_REPAIR_PRECONDITIONS

No recovery premise is inherent; any claim that depends on restoration must invoke explicit recovery postconditions.

MAINTAINABILITY_PRECONDITIONS

Access, modularity, safe isolation, procedures, test equipment, spares, tools, skills, logistics, configuration data and time under actual field conditions.

EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF

Prevents a superficially “accurate” detector from reducing total dependability.

DECISION_OR_CONSUMER

Operator, maintainer, service owner and automated-action authority.

KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES

Base-rate neglect; threshold gaming; alert suppression; label leakage; false alarm causing invasive maintenance; missed latent fault.

IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS

Monetising severe consequences can be ethically or practically difficult; uncertain costs can create false optimisation.

HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED

Single sensitivity targets evolved toward calibrated trade-offs, precision/recall by consequence, abstention and human-in-the-loop action.

MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM

A decision-calibrated coverage argument with uncertainty, base rates, action costs, drift checks and safe fallback.

CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY

An ROC/AUC chart or alert count is not the property; decision loss under the actual profile is.

RELIABILITY_EVIDENCE_PROFILE
APPLICABLE

true

PROPERTY

Explicit false-positive, false-negative and action cost

CLAIM

A decision-calibrated coverage argument with uncertainty, base rates, action costs, drift checks and safe fallback.

POPULATION_OR_DENOMINATOR

Declared units, missions, demands, requests, users, time windows or recurrent-event process.

CONFIGURATION_IDENTITY

Exact hardware, software, data, model, dependency and procedure versions.

OPERATIONAL_PROFILE

Representative demand/workload/environment with consequence-important rare cases identified.

TEST_OR_OBSERVATION_DESIGN

Predeclared sampling/censoring, failure scoring, exposure and stopping rules.

MODEL

Model selected for the stated estimand: life distribution, recurrent-event process, binomial demand, availability state model or direct functional check.

ASSUMPTIONS

Independence, stationarity, censoring, repair effectiveness, acceleration, coverage and representativeness are explicit.

CONFIDENCE_OR_UNCERTAINTY

Intervals, posterior uncertainty or bounded qualitative confidence; point estimates alone are insufficient.

CENSORING_AND_MISSINGNESS

Right/interval censoring, unreported events, lost telemetry and excluded windows are recorded.

COVERAGE

Claim coverage is limited to tested/observed failure modes, environments and paths.

DECISION_OR_CONSUMER

Operator, maintainer, service owner and automated-action authority.

CHEAP_PATH

Use a conservative rule or human confirmation when event rate is low and labels cannot support fine calibration.

CONTRARY_EVIDENCE

Where a direct deterministic functional check exists, statistical threshold optimisation is unnecessary.

EXPIRY_OR_FRESHNESS

Evidence expires when configuration, profile, environment, support organisation or dependencies materially change.

MATURE_FORM

A decision-calibrated coverage argument with uncertainty, base rates, action costs, drift checks and safe fallback.

DISPOSITION_RATIONALE

Admitted or rejected according to mechanism, trigger, assumption validity, evidence and anti-ceremony analysis.

ERM-P014 — Graceful degradation with explicit mission priorities
PROPERTY_ID

ERM-P014

PROPERTY_NAME

Graceful degradation with explicit mission priorities

HISTORICAL_ORIGIN

Fail-soft/fail-operational computing, multi-state reliability, survivability and service load shedding.

LINEAGE_CLASS

  1. FAULT_TOLERANT_COMPUTING_LINEAGE

  2. AVAILABILITY_AND_REPAIRABLE_SYSTEMS_LINEAGE

  3. SERVICE_RELIABILITY_OR_SRE_TRANSLATION

  4. HYBRID

RELATIONSHIP_TO_OTHER_TRADITIONS

  1. FAULT_TOLERANCE_IMPORT_OR_SHARED_ANCESTRY

  2. RELIABILITY_ENGINEERING_NATIVE

  3. DOMAIN_TRANSLATION

ORIGINAL_FORM

Continue with reduced throughput or disabled noncritical functions after faults.

PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED

Binary shutdown wastes residual capability, but uncontrolled “keep running” can hide unsafe, corrupt or inequitable service.

REQUIRED_FUNCTION_OR_MISSION

Preserve prioritised critical function within degraded performance, time and consequence limits until repair/recovery or safe stop.

FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT

The degraded state violates unstated dependencies, hides data loss, starves a user class, exceeds duration or cannot be exited.

MECHANISM

Predefine capability tiers, priorities, admission/load shedding, observability, annunciation, time limits, exit/escalation and recovery tests.

TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT

Capacity loss, partial dependency failure, redundancy exhaustion or missions where some function is better than none.

NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH

Stop safely when degraded operation has no defensible acceptance criteria or adds greater consequence than interruption.

DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS

Mission prioritisation, state model, resource reservation, independent observability, operator/user communication and recovery path.

OPERATIONAL_PROFILE_PRECONDITIONS

A declared and sufficiently representative operating profile, environment, duty cycle, mission phase and maintenance state.

FAILURE_MODEL_PRECONDITIONS

A failure model appropriate to the estimand; distinguish non-repairable life, recurrent repairable events, demand failures, software activation and service outcomes.

REDUNDANCY_OR_COMMON_CAUSE_PRECONDITIONS

No redundancy premise is needed; if redundancy appears in the implementation, common-cause and switchover assumptions must be analysed separately.

DIAGNOSTIC_COVERAGE_PRECONDITIONS

Observable functional outcomes sufficient to determine whether the claimed property actually holds.

RECOVERY_OR_REPAIR_PRECONDITIONS

A valid recovery source, authorised action, state/data reconciliation, dependency readiness and independently checked return-to-service postconditions.

MAINTAINABILITY_PRECONDITIONS

The design and support system must not make verification or corrective action infeasible.

EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF

Converts residual resources into protected critical service while bounding hidden deterioration.

DECISION_OR_CONSUMER

Mission owner, user populations, operator and incident commander.

KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES

Silent quality erosion; overload of remaining capacity; untested feature interactions; priority inversion; indefinite degraded mode.

IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS

“Graceful” is value-laden; degradation may shift harm to less visible consumers or conflict with safety/security constraints.

HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED

Fail-soft hardware evolved toward mission-tiered service, load shedding, partial availability and explicit degraded-state SLOs.

MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM

A tested degradation contract specifying protected functions, sacrificed functions, consumer impact, observability, duration and exit criteria.

CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY

A feature flag or “degraded mode” label is not the property; controlled consequence and exit are.

How should fairness and vulnerable-user consequences be built into degraded-service priorities?

ERM-P015 — Contextual selection of fail-safe, fail-stop and fail-operational objectives
PROPERTY_ID

ERM-P015

PROPERTY_NAME

Contextual selection of fail-safe, fail-stop and fail-operational objectives

HISTORICAL_ORIGIN

Protective engineering, fail-stop computing, fail-operational aerospace/transport systems and high-availability services.

LINEAGE_CLASS

  1. FAULT_TOLERANT_COMPUTING_LINEAGE

  2. DOMAIN_SPECIFIC

  3. HYBRID

RELATIONSHIP_TO_OTHER_TRADITIONS

  1. SAFETY_IMPORT_OR_SHARED_ANCESTRY

  2. FAULT_TOLERANCE_IMPORT_OR_SHARED_ANCESTRY

  3. UNRESOLVED_TENSION

ORIGINAL_FORM

Choose either a safe state on failure or continued operation through redundancy.

PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED

“Keep running” and “stop safely” protect different consequences and can conflict under uncertain state.

REQUIRED_FUNCTION_OR_MISSION

The action after fault must minimise mission/consumer consequence under the applicable safety, integrity, availability and recoverability priorities.

FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT

A universal availability objective continues corrupt/unsafe operation, or a universal stop policy destroys a time-critical mission that could tolerate degradation.

MECHANISM

Perform consequence- and mode-specific objective selection; define trigger, authority, state confidence, tolerated duration and fallback.

TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT

Functions where interruption and continued erroneous operation have materially different consequences.

NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH

Default to a simple safe stop for reversible low-consequence work when continued correctness cannot be established.

DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS

Consequence model, state observability, independent protection, degraded criteria and cross-discipline constraints.

OPERATIONAL_PROFILE_PRECONDITIONS

A declared and sufficiently representative operating profile, environment, duty cycle, mission phase and maintenance state.

FAILURE_MODEL_PRECONDITIONS

A failure model appropriate to the estimand; distinguish non-repairable life, recurrent repairable events, demand failures, software activation and service outcomes.

REDUNDANCY_OR_COMMON_CAUSE_PRECONDITIONS

No redundancy premise is needed; if redundancy appears in the implementation, common-cause and switchover assumptions must be analysed separately.

DIAGNOSTIC_COVERAGE_PRECONDITIONS

Observable functional outcomes sufficient to determine whether the claimed property actually holds.

RECOVERY_OR_REPAIR_PRECONDITIONS

No recovery premise is inherent; any claim that depends on restoration must invoke explicit recovery postconditions.

MAINTAINABILITY_PRECONDITIONS

The design and support system must not make verification or corrective action infeasible.

EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF

Prevents reliability optimisation from overriding more important mission consequences.

DECISION_OR_CONSUMER

Mission authority, operator, affected users and cross-functional assurance owners.

KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES

Ambiguous safe state; unsafe latent degradation; failover to corrupt state; emergency shutdown causes cascading loss.

IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS

No reliability-only rule resolves the value trade-off; evidence from one domain cannot set another domain’s objective.

HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED

Binary slogans evolved toward mode- and consequence-specific state machines with fail-operational intervals and escalation to stop.

MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM

A source-grounded, explicitly prioritised transition policy rather than a generic “always available” or “always fail safe” rule.

CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY

A “fail-safe” label is not the property; the consequence-based transition and evidence are.

CONTEXT_DEPENDENT

HIGH

  1. SRC-020

  2. SRC-028

  1. SRC-014

  3. SRC-056

  4. SRC-059

EMPIRICAL_OR_FIELD_EVIDENCE

Meta’s staged restoration avoided a potentially destabilising immediate return, showing recovery action must consider secondary consequence (SRC-059).

CONTRARY_EVIDENCE

Context determines which objective dominates; no general ranking was supported.

OPEN_QUESTIONS

How should authority be allocated when state confidence is low and safety, security and continuity owners disagree?

PROPERTY

Contextual selection of fail-safe, fail-stop and fail-operational objectives

REQUIRED_FUNCTION

The action after fault must minimise mission/consumer consequence under the applicable safety, integrity, availability and recoverability priorities.

MISSION_OR_SERVICE

The action after fault must minimise mission/consumer consequence under the applicable safety, integrity, availability and recoverability priorities.

OPERATING_PROFILE

Declared demands, workload mix, duty cycle, mode changes and maintenance states.

ENVIRONMENT

Declared physical, software, organisational and dependency environment.

FAILURE_DEFINITION

A universal availability objective continues corrupt/unsafe operation, or a universal stop policy destroys a time-critical mission that could tolerate degradation.

DEGRADED_ACCEPTABLE_STATE

Only states explicitly accepted by mission/consumer criteria; otherwise none.

MISSION_TIME_OR_DEMAND

Specified interval, number of demands or service-measurement window.

CONSUMER

Mission authority, operator, affected users and cross-functional assurance owners.

CHEAP_PATH

Default to a simple safe stop for reversible low-consequence work when continued correctness cannot be established.

MATURE_FORM

A source-grounded, explicitly prioritised transition policy rather than a generic “always available” or “always fail safe” rule.

PROPERTY

INITIATING_FAULT_OR_FAILURE

LOCAL_EFFECT

END_EFFECT

A universal availability objective continues corrupt/unsafe operation, or a universal stop policy destroys a time-critical mission that could tolerate degradation.

ERM-P016 — Recovery closes on required function and state postconditions
PROPERTY_ID

ERM-P016

PROPERTY_NAME

Recovery closes on required function and state postconditions

HISTORICAL_ORIGIN

Repairable-system restoration, recovery blocks, high-availability operations, recovery-oriented computing and modern reconstitution guidance.

LINEAGE_CLASS

  1. AVAILABILITY_AND_REPAIRABLE_SYSTEMS_LINEAGE

  2. FAULT_TOLERANT_COMPUTING_LINEAGE

  3. SERVICE_RELIABILITY_OR_SRE_TRANSLATION

  4. HYBRID

RELATIONSHIP_TO_OTHER_TRADITIONS

  1. RELIABILITY_ENGINEERING_NATIVE

  2. FAULT_TOLERANCE_IMPORT_OR_SHARED_ANCESTRY

  3. DOMAIN_TRANSLATION

  4. HYBRID_RESOLUTION

ORIGINAL_FORM

Count repair complete when the failed component is returned to an “up” state.

PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED

Restart, failover or repair may leave stale/corrupt state, unavailable dependencies, insufficient capacity or the initiating common cause.

REQUIRED_FUNCTION_OR_MISSION

Re-establish the ERM-P001 required function, authoritative state and dependency readiness within the declared restoration bound.

FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT

Process starts but user/mission function is wrong, incomplete, too slow, unauthorised or vulnerable to immediate recurrence.

MECHANISM

Define recovery source/authority, reconcile state, verify dependencies/capacity, perform independent end-to-end functional checks and control return to service.

TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT

Restart, retry, failover, rollback, restore, rebuild, repair, workaround or disaster recovery.

NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH

For stateless, low-consequence functions, a direct end-to-end transaction after restart may be sufficient.

DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS

Valid source, diagnosis/containment, state semantics, acceptance criteria, independent observability and fallback.

OPERATIONAL_PROFILE_PRECONDITIONS

A declared and sufficiently representative operating profile, environment, duty cycle, mission phase and maintenance state.

FAILURE_MODEL_PRECONDITIONS

A failure model appropriate to the estimand; distinguish non-repairable life, recurrent repairable events, demand failures, software activation and service outcomes.

REDUNDANCY_OR_COMMON_CAUSE_PRECONDITIONS

No redundancy premise is needed; if redundancy appears in the implementation, common-cause and switchover assumptions must be analysed separately.

DIAGNOSTIC_COVERAGE_PRECONDITIONS

Enumerated fault/failure population, observable signals, calibrated thresholds, isolation resolution, false-alarm and missed-detection costs.

RECOVERY_OR_REPAIR_PRECONDITIONS

A valid recovery source, authorised action, state/data reconciliation, dependency readiness and independently checked return-to-service postconditions.

MAINTAINABILITY_PRECONDITIONS

The design and support system must not make verification or corrective action infeasible.

EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF

Prevents “green after restart” from being mistaken for restoration and reduces recurrent/corrupt recovery.

DECISION_OR_CONSUMER

Operator, incident commander, mission owner, user and return-to-service authority.

KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES

Stale replica; incomplete replay; hidden dependency down; undercapacity; recurring trigger; invalid backup; monitor shares fault.

IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS

Exhaustive post-recovery validation can delay urgent service; checks must be risk- and time-proportionate.

HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED

Repair completion evolved from component-up to state-, dependency-, consumer- and mission-valid restoration.

MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM

A recovery case with explicit preconditions, authoritative state, functional/data/dependency postconditions, independent validation and fallback.

CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY

A closed incident or successful command is not the property; observed re-establishment of required function is.

CURRENT_STATUS

RECOVERY_RESTORATION_PROPERTY

MODERATE

MODERATE

VERY_HIGH

LOW_TO_MODERATE

HIGH

ASSUMPTION_SENSITIVITY

HIGH

CONTRARY_EVIDENCE_STRENGTH

HIGH

PRIMARY_SOURCES

  1. SRC-012

  2. SRC-020

  3. SRC-056

  4. SRC-057

  5. SRC-082

CRITICAL_SOURCES

  1. SRC-058

  2. SRC-059

  3. SRC-060

  4. SRC-078

EMPIRICAL_OR_FIELD_EVIDENCE

GitLab lost data despite recovery work; AWS performed integrity safety checks before full service; Meta staged restoration under load (SRC-058–SRC-060).

CONTRARY_EVIDENCE

For truly stateless/idempotent components, simple restart may be a valid cheap path—but that must be established, not assumed.

OPEN_QUESTIONS

What independent postcondition set is sufficient for complex services without making RTO unattainable?

APPLICABLE

true

PROPERTY

Recovery closes on required function and state postconditions

TARGET_FAULTS_FAILURES

Process starts but user/mission function is wrong, incomplete, too slow, unauthorised or vulnerable to immediate recurrence.

OBSERVABLES

Function outputs, timing, state, residuals, dependency signals and user/mission outcomes.

DETECTION_COVERAGE

Coverage is stated for an enumerated fault/failure population, operating mode and latency window.

FALSE_ALARM_COST

Unnecessary maintenance, failover, shutdown, operator load or customer disruption.

MISSED_DETECTION_COST

Continued propagation, latent damage, missed recovery window or false assurance.

ISOLATION_RESOLUTION

Replaceable unit, functional region, dependency or ambiguity group appropriate to the action.

AMBIGUITY_GROUPS

Plausible causes producing the same symptom are preserved until discriminated.

LATENCY

Detection and isolation time are bounded relative to propagation and recovery deadlines.

CONFIDENCE_CALIBRATION

Uncertainty, thresholds, drift and out-of-distribution conditions are exposed.

OPERATOR_OR_AUTOMATION_ACTION

Action is authority-bounded, reversible where feasible and matched to diagnosis confidence.

RECOVERY_COVERAGE

A detected fault counts only if the prescribed action restores or contains the target failure with known coverage.

MATURE_FORM

RECOVERY_ACTION

AUTHORITY_SOURCE

CHECKPOINT_BACKUP_SPARE_OR_ALTERNATE

RPO_OR_STATE_LOSS_BOUND

Explicit allowed state/data loss or “none” for state-continuous functions.

RTO_OR_RESTORATION_BOUND

FUNCTIONAL_POSTCONDITIONS

DEPENDENCY_POSTCONDITIONS

VALIDATION

Independent end-to-end functional and state checks under representative demand.

RETURN_TO_SERVICE_AUTHORITY

ROLLBACK_OR_FALLBACK

ERM-P017 — Backup, restore and reconstitution paths must be exercised and validated
PROPERTY_ID

ERM-P017

PROPERTY_NAME

Backup, restore and reconstitution paths must be exercised and validated

HISTORICAL_ORIGIN

Data backup/contingency practice, disaster recovery and current NIST restore/reconstitution guidance.

LINEAGE_CLASS

  1. MAINTAINABILITY_LINEAGE

  2. AVAILABILITY_AND_REPAIRABLE_SYSTEMS_LINEAGE

  3. SERVICE_RELIABILITY_OR_SRE_TRANSLATION

  4. HYBRID

RELATIONSHIP_TO_OTHER_TRADITIONS

  1. MAINTAINABILITY_NATIVE

  2. SERVICE_RELIABILITY_TRANSLATION

  3. HYBRID_RESOLUTION

ORIGINAL_FORM

Maintain a backup copy and a recovery plan.

PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED

Copies can be absent, stale, corrupt, inaccessible, incompatible, contaminated or impossible to restore within field constraints.

REQUIRED_FUNCTION_OR_MISSION

Restore required data/state and application function to a trusted, current-enough configuration within RPO/RTO and consequence limits.

FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT

Backup job reports success but restore cannot complete, recovered data is inconsistent, required tools/docs are unavailable or the same cause contaminates sources.

MECHANISM

Inventory sources, isolate/protect them, verify integrity/freshness, rehearse non-production restore, validate application semantics and reconstitution logistics.

TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT

Any claim of backup, disaster recovery, spare image, replicated state, rebuild-from-code or clean-room recovery.

NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH

For reproducible stateless artefacts, verify rebuild and one functional check instead of elaborate backup infrastructure.

DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS

Configuration manifests, credentials, tools, dependencies, capacity, state consistency, acceptance criteria and staff capability.

OPERATIONAL_PROFILE_PRECONDITIONS

A declared and sufficiently representative operating profile, environment, duty cycle, mission phase and maintenance state.

FAILURE_MODEL_PRECONDITIONS

A failure model appropriate to the estimand; distinguish non-repairable life, recurrent repairable events, demand failures, software activation and service outcomes.

REDUNDANCY_OR_COMMON_CAUSE_PRECONDITIONS

No redundancy premise is needed; if redundancy appears in the implementation, common-cause and switchover assumptions must be analysed separately.

DIAGNOSTIC_COVERAGE_PRECONDITIONS

Observable functional outcomes sufficient to determine whether the claimed property actually holds.

RECOVERY_OR_REPAIR_PRECONDITIONS

A valid recovery source, authorised action, state/data reconciliation, dependency readiness and independently checked return-to-service postconditions.

MAINTAINABILITY_PRECONDITIONS

Access, modularity, safe isolation, procedures, test equipment, spares, tools, skills, logistics, configuration data and time under actual field conditions.

EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF

Converts backup existence into demonstrated recoverability and exposes hidden RPO/RTO/support gaps.

DECISION_OR_CONSUMER

Service/data owner, operator, mission continuity authority and affected users.

KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES

Silent backup failure; restore never tested; missing encryption key; version mismatch; partial database; dependent identity/DNS unavailable; contaminated image.

IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS

Frequent full rehearsals are costly and can create production risk; representative, isolated exercises and cheaper checks should be chosen by decision value.

HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED

Backup media inventory evolved toward recurring restore, application/state validation, clean reconstitution and dependency/tool readiness.

MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM

A periodically refreshed restore/reconstitution demonstration closing on functional and state postconditions for the current configuration.

CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY

A backup icon, policy or DR document is not the property; a valid exercised recovery path is.

CURRENT_STATUS

RECOVERY_RESTORATION_PROPERTY

OPEN_QUESTIONS

How can restore evidence be maintained continuously for large stateful systems without prohibitive duplication?

RELIABILITY_EVIDENCE_PROFILE
APPLICABLE

true

PROPERTY

Backup, restore and reconstitution paths must be exercised and validated

CLAIM

A periodically refreshed restore/reconstitution demonstration closing on functional and state postconditions for the current configuration.

POPULATION_OR_DENOMINATOR

Declared units, missions, demands, requests, users, time windows or recurrent-event process.

CONFIGURATION_IDENTITY

Exact hardware, software, data, model, dependency and procedure versions.

OPERATIONAL_PROFILE

Representative demand/workload/environment with consequence-important rare cases identified.

TEST_OR_OBSERVATION_DESIGN

Predeclared sampling/censoring, failure scoring, exposure and stopping rules.

MODEL

Model selected for the stated estimand: life distribution, recurrent-event process, binomial demand, availability state model or direct functional check.

ASSUMPTIONS

Independence, stationarity, censoring, repair effectiveness, acceleration, coverage and representativeness are explicit.

CONFIDENCE_OR_UNCERTAINTY

Intervals, posterior uncertainty or bounded qualitative confidence; point estimates alone are insufficient.

CENSORING_AND_MISSINGNESS

Right/interval censoring, unreported events, lost telemetry and excluded windows are recorded.

COVERAGE

Claim coverage is limited to tested/observed failure modes, environments and paths.

DECISION_OR_CONSUMER

Service/data owner, operator, mission continuity authority and affected users.

CHEAP_PATH

For reproducible stateless artefacts, verify rebuild and one functional check instead of elaborate backup infrastructure.

CONTRARY_EVIDENCE

EXPIRY_OR_FRESHNESS

MATURE_FORM

A periodically refreshed restore/reconstitution demonstration closing on functional and state postconditions for the current configuration.

DISPOSITION_RATIONALE

Admitted or rejected according to mechanism, trigger, assumption validity, evidence and anti-ceremony analysis.

ERM-P018 — Conditional validity of retry, restart, rollback and checkpoint recovery
PROPERTY_ID

ERM-P018

PROPERTY_NAME

Conditional validity of retry, restart, rollback and checkpoint recovery

HISTORICAL_ORIGIN

Recovery blocks, checkpoint/restart, transaction rollback and high-availability operations.

LINEAGE_CLASS

  1. FAULT_TOLERANT_COMPUTING_LINEAGE

  2. SOFTWARE_RELIABILITY_LINEAGE

  3. SERVICE_RELIABILITY_OR_SRE_TRANSLATION

  4. HYBRID

RELATIONSHIP_TO_OTHER_TRADITIONS

  1. FAULT_TOLERANCE_IMPORT_OR_SHARED_ANCESTRY

  2. DOMAIN_TRANSLATION

  3. HYBRID_RESOLUTION

ORIGINAL_FORM

Retry a failed operation, restart the process, roll back the version or resume from a checkpoint.

PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED

Recovery primitives are beneficial for transient/local faults but amplify overload, repeat deterministic faults or restore inconsistent state when assumptions fail.

REQUIRED_FUNCTION_OR_MISSION

Complete the intended operation or restore service/state without duplicate side effects, overload, corruption or recurrence.

FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT

Retry storm, non-idempotent duplicate, domino rollback, stale checkpoint, incompatible rollback, restart loop or hidden dependency failure.

MECHANISM

Classify fault transience; bound retries/backoff; establish idempotency; coordinate checkpoints; validate rollback compatibility; circuit-break and escalate.

TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT

Transient faults, process crashes, failed releases or recoverable state loss where operation semantics and state are known.

NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH

Do not retry deterministic validation errors or overload; fail clearly and preserve evidence.

DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS

Idempotency/deduplication, state consistency, checkpoint freshness, dependency capacity, rollback artefacts and stop conditions.

OPERATIONAL_PROFILE_PRECONDITIONS

A declared and sufficiently representative operating profile, environment, duty cycle, mission phase and maintenance state.

FAILURE_MODEL_PRECONDITIONS

A failure model appropriate to the estimand; distinguish non-repairable life, recurrent repairable events, demand failures, software activation and service outcomes.

REDUNDANCY_OR_COMMON_CAUSE_PRECONDITIONS

Credible failure-domain independence, common-cause analysis, standby condition, switchover coverage and state compatibility.

DIAGNOSTIC_COVERAGE_PRECONDITIONS

Observable functional outcomes sufficient to determine whether the claimed property actually holds.

RECOVERY_OR_REPAIR_PRECONDITIONS

A valid recovery source, authorised action, state/data reconciliation, dependency readiness and independently checked return-to-service postconditions.

MAINTAINABILITY_PRECONDITIONS

The design and support system must not make verification or corrective action infeasible.

EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF

Shortens restoration for the subset of failures these primitives actually cover.

DECISION_OR_CONSUMER

Service owner, operator, recovery controller and users.

KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES

Retries increase load; checkpoint overhead; uncoordinated state; old schema cannot roll back; automated loop destroys evidence.

IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS

Recovery primitives can mask design defects and create correlated cascades; their success rate is workload- and fault-specific.

HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED

Unconditional restart evolved toward bounded, idempotent, state-aware recovery with escalation and postcondition validation.

MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM

A fault-class-specific recovery policy with bounded attempts, consistency/compatibility proof, load protection, observation and fallback.

CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY

A restart button, retry library or checkpoint file is not the property; valid semantics and successful postconditions are.

  1. SRC-058

  3. SRC-060

  4. SRC-066

EMPIRICAL_OR_FIELD_EVIDENCE

AWS accumulated backlogs and staged recovery; Meta avoided all-at-once restart; GitLab’s restore source was incomplete (SRC-058–SRC-060).

CONTRARY_EVIDENCE

For independent transient faults in stateless/idempotent operations, retry/restart has strong practical value.

OPEN_QUESTIONS

How should automated recovery estimate when a fault is transient versus systematic under sparse evidence?

MISSION_RELIABILITY_PROFILE
APPLICABLE

true

PROPERTY

Conditional validity of retry, restart, rollback and checkpoint recovery

REQUIRED_FUNCTION

Complete the intended operation or restore service/state without duplicate side effects, overload, corruption or recurrence.

MISSION_OR_SERVICE

Complete the intended operation or restore service/state without duplicate side effects, overload, corruption or recurrence.

OPERATING_PROFILE

Declared demands, workload mix, duty cycle, mode changes and maintenance states.

ENVIRONMENT

Declared physical, software, organisational and dependency environment.

FAILURE_DEFINITION

Retry storm, non-idempotent duplicate, domino rollback, stale checkpoint, incompatible rollback, restart loop or hidden dependency failure.

DEGRADED_ACCEPTABLE_STATE

Only states explicitly accepted by mission/consumer criteria; otherwise none.

MISSION_TIME_OR_DEMAND

Specified interval, number of demands or service-measurement window.

CONSUMER

Service owner, operator, recovery controller and users.

CHEAP_PATH

Do not retry deterministic validation errors or overload; fail clearly and preserve evidence.

MATURE_FORM

A fault-class-specific recovery policy with bounded attempts, consistency/compatibility proof, load protection, observation and fallback.

FAILURE_PROPAGATION_PROFILE
APPLICABLE

true

PROPERTY

Conditional validity of retry, restart, rollback and checkpoint recovery

INITIATING_FAULT_OR_FAILURE

Retry storm, non-idempotent duplicate, domino rollback, stale checkpoint, incompatible rollback, restart loop or hidden dependency failure.

LOCAL_EFFECT

Loss, corruption, delay, false indication or degraded performance at the initiating item.

NEXT_LEVEL_EFFECT

Effect on the enclosing function, dependency path or maintenance process.

END_EFFECT

Retry storm, non-idempotent duplicate, domino rollback, stale checkpoint, incompatible rollback, restart loop or hidden dependency failure.

PROPAGATION_PATH

Functional, physical, state, timing, control, resource or organisational coupling.

CONTAINMENT_BOUNDARY

A declared fault-containment region or explicit stop/degradation boundary.

DEPENDENCIES

Idempotency/deduplication, state consistency, checkpoint freshness, dependency capacity, rollback artefacts and stop conditions.

COMMON_CAUSE_OR_SHARED_COUPLING

Shared specification, environment, supplier, control plane, state, maintenance or operator path must be challenged.

LATENT_CONDITION

Standby, monitoring, repair or dependency defects may remain latent until demand.

DETECTION

Outcome- or function-relevant observation, not a component heartbeat alone.

CONTROL

Classify fault transience; bound retries/backoff; establish idempotency; coordinate checkpoints; validate rollback compatibility; circuit-break and escalate.

RESIDUAL_RISK

Unmodelled combinations, dependence, coverage gaps and changed configuration remain.

REDUNDANCY_COMMON_CAUSE_PROFILE
APPLICABLE

true

PROPERTY

Conditional validity of retry, restart, rollback and checkpoint recovery

REDUNDANCY_FORM

Active, standby, voting, diverse, replicated or alternate-path redundancy as explicitly declared.

CLAIMED_TOLERANCE

The specified number/class of faults tolerated while meeting required function or an accepted degraded state.

FAILURE_DOMAINS

Physical, power, network, control-plane, state, software, supplier, operator and maintenance domains.

INDEPENDENCE_EVIDENCE

Evidence must address shared causes rather than infer independence from copy count or labels.

COMMON_CAUSE_COUPLINGS

Specification, design, implementation, environment, supplier, maintenance, configuration and operational coupling.

STANDBY_LATENCY_OR_DORMANCY

Dormant faults, activation delay and cold/warm/hot readiness are measured when applicable.

SWITCHOVER_COVERAGE

Detection, decision, transfer, state synchronisation and post-transfer functional success.

STATE_CONSISTENCY

The takeover state and authority must be current, complete and semantically valid.

DEGRADED_MODE

Declared reduced capability and exit conditions.

TEST_OR_FIELD_EVIDENCE

Representative failover/failure tests plus field or incident evidence where available.

MATURE_FORM

A fault-class-specific recovery policy with bounded attempts, consistency/compatibility proof, load protection, observation and fallback.

DIAGNOSTIC_COVERAGE_PROFILE
APPLICABLE

false

PROFILE_TYPE

DIAGNOSTIC_COVERAGE_PROFILE

REASON

The candidate is not a diagnostic claim; outcome observation may still be required by its evidence profile.

RECOVERY_RESTORATION_PROFILE
APPLICABLE

true

PROPERTY

Conditional validity of retry, restart, rollback and checkpoint recovery

INITIATING_FAILURE

Retry storm, non-idempotent duplicate, domino rollback, stale checkpoint, incompatible rollback, restart loop or hidden dependency failure.

RECOVERY_ACTION

Classify fault transience; bound retries/backoff; establish idempotency; coordinate checkpoints; validate rollback compatibility; circuit-break and escalate.

AUTHORITY_SOURCE

The designated current configuration/state authority and authorised operator or controller.

CHECKPOINT_BACKUP_SPARE_OR_ALTERNATE

Declared checkpoint, replica, backup, spare, rebuild source or manual workaround.

RPO_OR_STATE_LOSS_BOUND

Explicit allowed state/data loss or “none” for state-continuous functions.

RTO_OR_RESTORATION_BOUND

Explicit time-to-restoration bound including diagnosis, logistics, repair and validation.

STATE_RECONCILIATION

Resolve divergent, stale, partial or replayed state before authoritative service resumes.

FUNCTIONAL_POSTCONDITIONS

Complete the intended operation or restore service/state without duplicate side effects, overload, corruption or recurrence.

DATA_STATE_POSTCONDITIONS

Integrity, completeness, freshness, ordering and authority appropriate to the mission.

DEPENDENCY_POSTCONDITIONS

Critical dependencies and capacity are verified rather than assumed from local restart.

VALIDATION

Independent end-to-end functional and state checks under representative demand.

RETURN_TO_SERVICE_AUTHORITY

Named human or automated authority with explicit acceptance criteria.

ROLLBACK_OR_FALLBACK

A bounded fallback when restoration validation fails or the original cause persists.

ERM-P019 — Repairability and maintainability by design
PROPERTY_ID

ERM-P019

PROPERTY_NAME

Repairability and maintainability by design

HISTORICAL_ORIGIN

Post-war maintainability programmes, maintainability prediction/demonstration and modular field replacement.

LINEAGE_CLASS

  1. MAINTAINABILITY_LINEAGE

  2. AVAILABILITY_AND_REPAIRABLE_SYSTEMS_LINEAGE

  3. CONVERGENT_ENGINEERING

RELATIONSHIP_TO_OTHER_TRADITIONS

  1. MAINTAINABILITY_NATIVE

  2. RELIABILITY_ENGINEERING_NATIVE

  3. CONVERGENT_PROPERTY

ORIGINAL_FORM

Reduce mean corrective maintenance time through modularity, accessibility and standard repair tasks.

PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED

A reliable design can impose unacceptable downtime or recurring error because faults cannot be isolated, accessed, repaired or verified in the field.

REQUIRED_FUNCTION_OR_MISSION

Restore the defined function safely within the total downtime bound under actual field support conditions.

FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT

Procedure exists but access, isolation, tools, spares, skills, state/configuration transfer or verification make repair infeasible or error-prone.

MECHANISM

Design for access, isolation, modular replacement, test points, diagnostics, standard interfaces, safe handling, configuration/state transfer and repair verification.

TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT

Repairable/high-value/long-life systems, remote sites, tight restoration bounds, repeated service intervention or obsolescence exposure.

NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH

For low-cost short-life items, replacement or graceful discard may dominate elaborate repairability.

DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS

Maintenance concept, field observations, human factors, logistics, tools, documentation, configuration control and testability.

OPERATIONAL_PROFILE_PRECONDITIONS

A declared and sufficiently representative operating profile, environment, duty cycle, mission phase and maintenance state.

FAILURE_MODEL_PRECONDITIONS

A failure model appropriate to the estimand; distinguish non-repairable life, recurrent repairable events, demand failures, software activation and service outcomes.

REDUNDANCY_OR_COMMON_CAUSE_PRECONDITIONS

No redundancy premise is needed; if redundancy appears in the implementation, common-cause and switchover assumptions must be analysed separately.

DIAGNOSTIC_COVERAGE_PRECONDITIONS

Enumerated fault/failure population, observable signals, calibrated thresholds, isolation resolution, false-alarm and missed-detection costs.

RECOVERY_OR_REPAIR_PRECONDITIONS

A valid recovery source, authorised action, state/data reconciliation, dependency readiness and independently checked return-to-service postconditions.

MAINTAINABILITY_PRECONDITIONS

Access, modularity, safe isolation, procedures, test equipment, spares, tools, skills, logistics, configuration data and time under actual field conditions.

EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF

Reduces active repair and error while making reliability growth and lifecycle support feasible.

DECISION_OR_CONSUMER

Maintainer, operator, logistics owner, asset owner and service user.

KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES

Modularity moves complexity to interfaces; inaccessible fasteners; ambiguous connectors; replacement loses calibration/state; “no fault found.”

IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS

Optimising predicted MTTR can ignore diagnosis, logistics and validation; modularity can add failure points.

HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED

Task-time prediction evolved toward maintainability-by-design, testability, software operability, remote support and total restoration burden.

MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM

Field-demonstrated restoration capability spanning detection, access, repair/replacement, configuration/state preservation, validation and return to service.

CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY

A maintainability prediction is not the property; repair performed correctly under real support constraints is.

MAINTAINABILITY_PROPERTY

  3. SRC-057

  4. SRC-076

  5. SRC-077

EMPIRICAL_OR_FIELD_EVIDENCE

Meta’s need for physical onsite access and GitLab’s undocumented/manual repair path show design and organisational support dominate restoration (SRC-059, SRC-060).

CONTRARY_EVIDENCE

For disposable or non-repairable items, maintainability may not trigger; replacement/logistics still require analysis.

OPEN_QUESTIONS

How can software state/configuration portability be treated as a first-class maintainability attribute across generations?

DIAGNOSTIC_COVERAGE_PROFILE
APPLICABLE

true

PROPERTY

Repairability and maintainability by design

TARGET_FAULTS_FAILURES

Procedure exists but access, isolation, tools, spares, skills, state/configuration transfer or verification make repair infeasible or error-prone.

OBSERVABLES

Function outputs, timing, state, residuals, dependency signals and user/mission outcomes.

DETECTION_COVERAGE

Coverage is stated for an enumerated fault/failure population, operating mode and latency window.

FALSE_ALARM_COST

Unnecessary maintenance, failover, shutdown, operator load or customer disruption.

MISSED_DETECTION_COST

Continued propagation, latent damage, missed recovery window or false assurance.

ISOLATION_RESOLUTION

Replaceable unit, functional region, dependency or ambiguity group appropriate to the action.

AMBIGUITY_GROUPS

Plausible causes producing the same symptom are preserved until discriminated.

LATENCY

Detection and isolation time are bounded relative to propagation and recovery deadlines.

CONFIDENCE_CALIBRATION

Uncertainty, thresholds, drift and out-of-distribution conditions are exposed.

OPERATOR_OR_AUTOMATION_ACTION

Action is authority-bounded, reversible where feasible and matched to diagnosis confidence.

RECOVERY_COVERAGE

A detected fault counts only if the prescribed action restores or contains the target failure with known coverage.

MATURE_FORM

Field-demonstrated restoration capability spanning detection, access, repair/replacement, configuration/state preservation, validation and return to service.

ERM-P020 — Supportability, logistics and operational maintenance capability
PROPERTY_ID

ERM-P020

PROPERTY_NAME

Supportability, logistics and operational maintenance capability

HISTORICAL_ORIGIN

Integrated logistics support, operational availability and current IEC supportability practice.

LINEAGE_CLASS

  1. MAINTAINABILITY_LINEAGE

  2. AVAILABILITY_AND_REPAIRABLE_SYSTEMS_LINEAGE

  3. HYBRID

RELATIONSHIP_TO_OTHER_TRADITIONS

  1. MAINTAINABILITY_NATIVE

  2. RELIABILITY_ENGINEERING_NATIVE

  3. HYBRID_RESOLUTION

ORIGINAL_FORM

Provision spares, tools, manuals, trained maintainers and maintenance facilities to meet downtime targets.

PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED

A maintainable design remains unavailable because diagnosis, administration, transport, spares, credentials, tools, documentation or personnel are delayed.

REQUIRED_FUNCTION_OR_MISSION

The complete support system restores required function within the operational downtime bound across deployed locations and mission conditions.

FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT

Low bench repair time is overwhelmed by waiting, access, approval, supplier lead time, obsolete parts, unavailable expertise or incompatible configuration.

MECHANISM

Model and measure total downtime; provision/position resources; exercise escalation; manage obsolescence; sustain documentation, credentials and skills.

TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT

Distributed fleets, remote/contested sites, long-life assets, supplier concentration, strict availability or disaster recovery.

NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH

For low-criticality items, accept longer restoration or stock simple replacements rather than build a complex support network.

DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS

Demand rates, repair-level policy, supply data, workforce readiness, transport/access, contracts, configuration and obsolescence monitoring.

OPERATIONAL_PROFILE_PRECONDITIONS

A declared and sufficiently representative operating profile, environment, duty cycle, mission phase and maintenance state.

FAILURE_MODEL_PRECONDITIONS

A failure model appropriate to the estimand; distinguish non-repairable life, recurrent repairable events, demand failures, software activation and service outcomes.

REDUNDANCY_OR_COMMON_CAUSE_PRECONDITIONS

No redundancy premise is needed; if redundancy appears in the implementation, common-cause and switchover assumptions must be analysed separately.

DIAGNOSTIC_COVERAGE_PRECONDITIONS

Observable functional outcomes sufficient to determine whether the claimed property actually holds.

RECOVERY_OR_REPAIR_PRECONDITIONS

A valid recovery source, authorised action, state/data reconciliation, dependency readiness and independently checked return-to-service postconditions.

MAINTAINABILITY_PRECONDITIONS

Access, modularity, safe isolation, procedures, test equipment, spares, tools, skills, logistics, configuration data and time under actual field conditions.

EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF

Aligns achieved/operational availability with real support conditions rather than inherent design promises.

DECISION_OR_CONSUMER

Fleet/asset owner, logistics organisation, operator, maintainer and mission planner.

KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES

Paper spares; wrong revision; expired credentials; inaccessible facility; unsupported dependency; concentrated specialist; supply disruption.

IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS

Stocking and spare capacity are costly; forecasts are uncertain; overprovisioning can create obsolescence and maintenance burden.

HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED

Spares provisioning broadened to lifecycle supportability, digital/software dependencies, remote operations and obsolescence management.

MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM

A tested support concept with total-downtime data, resource/configuration identity, alternatives and explicit risk acceptance for uncovered sites/times.

CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY

A logistics plan or SLA is not the property; actual timely access to the right resources is.

CONTRARY_EVIDENCE_STRENGTH

MODERATE

  1. SRC-009

  3. SRC-032

  4. SRC-036

  5. SRC-038

CRITICAL_SOURCES

  1. SRC-056

  2. SRC-057

  3. SRC-059

  4. SRC-060

  5. SRC-077

EMPIRICAL_OR_FIELD_EVIDENCE

Meta’s secure physical access delayed repair; GitLab relied on slower infrastructure in another region; both show logistics/access are reliability mechanisms (SRC-059, SRC-060).

CONTRARY_EVIDENCE

High reliability or local repairability can reduce support demand, but does not eliminate the need to validate residual support assumptions.

OPEN_QUESTIONS

How should supply-chain and workforce correlation be integrated with traditional stochastic availability models?

ERM-P021 — Maintenance-induced failure and maintenance human factors as system mechanisms
PROPERTY_ID

ERM-P021

PROPERTY_NAME

Maintenance-induced failure and maintenance human factors as system mechanisms

HISTORICAL_ORIGIN

Field maintenance experience, RCM, human-factors programmes and common-cause analysis.

LINEAGE_CLASS

  1. MAINTAINABILITY_LINEAGE

  2. FAILURE_ANALYSIS_LINEAGE

  3. HYBRID

RELATIONSHIP_TO_OTHER_TRADITIONS

  1. MAINTAINABILITY_NATIVE

  2. RELIABILITY_ENGINEERING_NATIVE

  3. CONVERGENT_PROPERTY

ORIGINAL_FORM

Record maintenance error as a cause category and improve procedures/training.

PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED

Inspection, calibration, replacement, access, configuration or repair can introduce faults, disable redundancy or create shared misconfiguration.

REQUIRED_FUNCTION_OR_MISSION

Maintenance must preserve or restore the required function without creating a new latent, common-cause or interface failure.

FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT

Wrong part/configuration, omitted step, damage during access, common calibration error, simultaneous channel disablement or ineffective verification.

MECHANISM

Design out error opportunities; control configuration; isolate channels; use usable procedures/tools; manage fatigue/communication; verify function after work; learn without individual blame substitution.

TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT

Intrusive maintenance, repeated “no fault found,” common-channel work, difficult access, night/remote work, complex software/configuration changes.

NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH

Eliminate unnecessary maintenance or replace a fragile manual step with a simple poka-yoke/functional check where that is safer.

DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS

Work-as-done observation, maintainability design, staffing/fatigue controls, independent verification, event reporting and organisational learning.

OPERATIONAL_PROFILE_PRECONDITIONS

A declared and sufficiently representative operating profile, environment, duty cycle, mission phase and maintenance state.

FAILURE_MODEL_PRECONDITIONS

A failure model appropriate to the estimand; distinguish non-repairable life, recurrent repairable events, demand failures, software activation and service outcomes.

REDUNDANCY_OR_COMMON_CAUSE_PRECONDITIONS

No redundancy premise is needed; if redundancy appears in the implementation, common-cause and switchover assumptions must be analysed separately.

DIAGNOSTIC_COVERAGE_PRECONDITIONS

Enumerated fault/failure population, observable signals, calibrated thresholds, isolation resolution, false-alarm and missed-detection costs.

RECOVERY_OR_REPAIR_PRECONDITIONS

No recovery premise is inherent; any claim that depends on restoration must invoke explicit recovery postconditions.

MAINTAINABILITY_PRECONDITIONS

Access, modularity, safe isolation, procedures, test equipment, spares, tools, skills, logistics, configuration data and time under actual field conditions.

EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF

Reduces recurrence, common-cause exposure and downtime produced by the maintenance system itself.

DECISION_OR_CONSUMER

Maintainer, operator, fleet/service owner and mission authority.

KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES

Blame-only investigation; unreadable procedures; undocumented workaround; verifier shares same misconception; work performed on both redundant channels.

IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS

“Human error” can become a residual label that hides design and organisational causes; added checks can create workload and new failure modes.

HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED

Person-centred error counting evolved toward design-for-maintenance, organisational conditions, independent verification and work-as-done evidence.

MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM

Maintenance treated as an operational mode with its own hazards, dependencies, state transitions, common-cause controls and post-maintenance functional proof.

CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY

A sign-off or training record is not the property; reduced error opportunity and verified post-work function are.

EMPIRICAL_OR_FIELD_EVIDENCE

AWS’s event began with an authorised maintenance command whose input removed excess capacity; GitLab’s recovery work accidentally removed the primary data directory (SRC-058, SRC-060).

CONTRARY_EVIDENCE

Some maintenance errors are genuinely individual slips, but durable control still requires analysing the conditions and barriers around them.

OPEN_QUESTIONS

Which post-maintenance checks best detect semantic/configuration errors without duplicating the entire mission test?

PROPERTY

Maintenance-induced failure and maintenance human factors as system mechanisms

INITIATING_FAULT_OR_FAILURE

Wrong part/configuration, omitted step, damage during access, common calibration error, simultaneous channel disablement or ineffective verification.

LOCAL_EFFECT

Loss, corruption, delay, false indication or degraded performance at the initiating item.

NEXT_LEVEL_EFFECT

Effect on the enclosing function, dependency path or maintenance process.

END_EFFECT

Wrong part/configuration, omitted step, damage during access, common calibration error, simultaneous channel disablement or ineffective verification.

PROPAGATION_PATH

Functional, physical, state, timing, control, resource or organisational coupling.

CONTAINMENT_BOUNDARY

A declared fault-containment region or explicit stop/degradation boundary.

DETECTION

Continued propagation, latent damage, missed recovery window or false assurance.

ISOLATION_RESOLUTION

Replaceable unit, functional region, dependency or ambiguity group appropriate to the action.

AMBIGUITY_GROUPS

Plausible causes producing the same symptom are preserved until discriminated.

LATENCY

Detection and isolation time are bounded relative to propagation and recovery deadlines.

CONFIDENCE_CALIBRATION

Uncertainty, thresholds, drift and out-of-distribution conditions are exposed.

RECOVERY_COVERAGE

A detected fault counts only if the prescribed action restores or contains the target failure with known coverage.

ERM-P022 — Age-based preventive maintenance only for valid age-related mechanisms
PROPERTY_ID

ERM-P022

PROPERTY_NAME

Age-based preventive maintenance only for valid age-related mechanisms

HISTORICAL_ORIGIN

Replacement theory and scheduled overhaul; narrowed by airline RCM evidence and modern life-data modelling.

LINEAGE_CLASS

  1. LIFE_DATA_AND_STATISTICAL_RELIABILITY_LINEAGE

  2. MAINTAINABILITY_LINEAGE

  3. HYBRID

RELATIONSHIP_TO_OTHER_TRADITIONS

  1. STATISTICAL_RELIABILITY_ANCESTRY

  2. MAINTAINABILITY_NATIVE

  3. HYBRID_RESOLUTION

ORIGINAL_FORM

Replace or overhaul at a fixed age to avoid wear-out.

PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED

Scheduled intervention cannot reduce random/weakly age-related failures and may introduce maintenance faults or waste useful life.

REQUIRED_FUNCTION_OR_MISSION

Reduce mission failure or lifecycle consequence by acting before a demonstrable age-related risk rise.

FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT

A convenient age limit is imposed without a dominant wear-out mode, stable age relationship or evidence that overhaul restores resistance.

MECHANISM

Identify failure mechanism and conditional age-risk; estimate survival/uncertainty; compare maintenance-induced risk and cost; verify post-task reliability.

TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT

Dominant wear-out, fatigue, corrosion, consumable depletion or legally/physically bounded life with observable age/exposure.

NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH

Run to failure, inspect condition or use simple replacement-on-failure when consequence and restoration support permit.

DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS

Mechanism-specific life data, exposure rather than calendar age where appropriate, restoration-effect evidence and maintenance quality.

OPERATIONAL_PROFILE_PRECONDITIONS

A declared and sufficiently representative operating profile, environment, duty cycle, mission phase and maintenance state.

FAILURE_MODEL_PRECONDITIONS

A failure model appropriate to the estimand; distinguish non-repairable life, recurrent repairable events, demand failures, software activation and service outcomes.

REDUNDANCY_OR_COMMON_CAUSE_PRECONDITIONS

No redundancy premise is needed; if redundancy appears in the implementation, common-cause and switchover assumptions must be analysed separately.

DIAGNOSTIC_COVERAGE_PRECONDITIONS

Observable functional outcomes sufficient to determine whether the claimed property actually holds.

RECOVERY_OR_REPAIR_PRECONDITIONS

No recovery premise is inherent; any claim that depends on restoration must invoke explicit recovery postconditions.

MAINTAINABILITY_PRECONDITIONS

Access, modularity, safe isolation, procedures, test equipment, spares, tools, skills, logistics, configuration data and time under actual field conditions.

EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF

Prevents predictable wear-out while avoiding ritual overhaul of items without useful age limits.

DECISION_OR_CONSUMER

Maintenance planner, asset/fleet owner and mission-risk authority.

KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES

Calendar age used instead of cycles/stress; mixture populations; changed materials; overhaul not as-good-as-new; invasive task causes failure.

IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS

The famous RCM pattern proportions are aircraft-specific; they justify mechanism testing, not a universal claim that age never matters.

HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED

Universal scheduled overhaul narrowed to mechanism- and consequence-specific age tasks with field-data refresh.

MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM

A maintenance interval justified by a causal degradation mechanism, exposure model, uncertainty, task effectiveness and net-consequence comparison.

CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY

A preventive-maintenance calendar is not the property; intervention before a validated age-linked risk is.

CURRENT_STATUS

ASSUMPTION_SENSITIVE

HIGH

MODERATE

MODERATE

OPERATIONAL_PRACTICE_STRENGTH

MODERATE

REPLICATION_OR_MULTI_STUDY_STRENGTH

MODERATE

TRANSFERABILITY_STRENGTH

CONTEXT_DEPENDENT

ASSUMPTION_SENSITIVITY

CONTRARY_EVIDENCE_STRENGTH

HIGH

PRIMARY_SOURCES

  1. SRC-008

  2. SRC-021

  3. SRC-022

  4. SRC-034

  5. SRC-040

CRITICAL_SOURCES

  1. SRC-041

  2. SRC-046

  3. SRC-076

EMPIRICAL_OR_FIELD_EVIDENCE

Nowlan and Heap found multiple age-reliability patterns and set applicability conditions for scheduled rework rather than endorsing universal intervals (SRC-008).

CONTRARY_EVIDENCE

Strong wear-out mechanisms and finite-life items provide robust contexts where age-based replacement is retained.

OPEN_QUESTIONS

How should sparse field data be pooled across configurations without erasing mechanism differences?

Declared units, missions, demands, requests, users, time windows or recurrent-event process.

ERM-P023 — Condition-based and predictive maintenance only with observable precursors and decision value
PROPERTY_ID

ERM-P023

PROPERTY_NAME

Condition-based and predictive maintenance only with observable precursors and decision value

HISTORICAL_ORIGIN

On-condition RCM, machinery condition monitoring, PHM and modern ML/digital-twin predictive maintenance.

LINEAGE_CLASS

  1. DIAGNOSTICS_AND_PHM_LINEAGE

  2. MAINTAINABILITY_LINEAGE

  3. DOMAIN_SPECIFIC

  4. HYBRID

RELATIONSHIP_TO_OTHER_TRADITIONS

  1. MAINTAINABILITY_NATIVE

  2. STATISTICAL_RELIABILITY_ANCESTRY

  3. DOMAIN_TRANSLATION

  4. HYBRID_RESOLUTION

ORIGINAL_FORM

Inspect a condition indicator before the P–F interval expires or predict remaining useful life to schedule work.

PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED

Fixed schedules waste life, but predictions can be uncalibrated, late, false-positive or non-transferable and can trigger harmful maintenance.

REQUIRED_FUNCTION_OR_MISSION

Detect actionable degradation with enough lead time and confidence that intervention reduces expected mission/lifecycle loss.

FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT

No stable precursor; sensor drift; domain shift; uncertain RUL ignored; maintenance action ineffective; false alarms dominate; lead time shorter than logistics delay.

MECHANISM

Validate precursor-to-failure relation, sensor chain, uncertainty, lead-time distribution and intervention effect; optimise against simple policies; monitor drift.

TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT

Failure mechanisms with measurable deterioration, sufficient lead time, meaningful consequence and an effective intervention.

NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH

Use inspection, fixed replacement or run-to-failure when a simple policy performs similarly or data/precursors are inadequate.

DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS

Reliable sensing, labelled/field failure data, calibrated uncertainty, domain representativeness, maintenance capacity and action-cost model.

OPERATIONAL_PROFILE_PRECONDITIONS

A declared and sufficiently representative operating profile, environment, duty cycle, mission phase and maintenance state.

FAILURE_MODEL_PRECONDITIONS

A failure model appropriate to the estimand; distinguish non-repairable life, recurrent repairable events, demand failures, software activation and service outcomes.

REDUNDANCY_OR_COMMON_CAUSE_PRECONDITIONS

No redundancy premise is needed; if redundancy appears in the implementation, common-cause and switchover assumptions must be analysed separately.

DIAGNOSTIC_COVERAGE_PRECONDITIONS

Enumerated fault/failure population, observable signals, calibrated thresholds, isolation resolution, false-alarm and missed-detection costs.

RECOVERY_OR_REPAIR_PRECONDITIONS

No recovery premise is inherent; any claim that depends on restoration must invoke explicit recovery postconditions.

MAINTAINABILITY_PRECONDITIONS

Access, modularity, safe isolation, procedures, test equipment, spares, tools, skills, logistics, configuration data and time under actual field conditions.

EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF

Targets intervention where it can avert failure while reducing needless planned maintenance.

DECISION_OR_CONSUMER

Maintenance planner, asset owner, operator and logistics manager.

KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES

Benchmark overfit; synthetic-data gap; leakage; censored labels; black-box confidence; alert fatigue; action changes the data-generating process.

IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS

The literature is heterogeneous and often laboratory/dataset-centric; no evidence supports universal superiority over simpler policies.

HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED

Threshold condition monitoring expanded to probabilistic PHM, hybrid physics/data models, uncertainty and decision-aware maintenance.

MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM

A field-calibrated, uncertainty-aware precursor and intervention case compared against a cheap baseline under the actual loss function.

CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY

A “predictive maintenance” platform or RUL number is not the property; demonstrated earlier/better decisions are.

CURRENT_STATUS

  6. SRC-072

  1. SRC-040

  3. SRC-073

  4. SRC-074

EMPIRICAL_OR_FIELD_EVIDENCE

Systematic reviews show rapid growth but heterogeneous applications; the 2026 PHM review treats uncertainty as unavoidable throughout deployment (SRC-071–SRC-073).

CONTRARY_EVIDENCE

Well-instrumented monotonic degradation with proven intervention can yield strong value; the result remains mechanism-specific.

OPEN_QUESTIONS

How often do published gains survive cross-site transfer and prospective comparison with simple maintenance rules?

ERM-P024 — Closed-loop test–analyse–fix and FRACAS
PROPERTY_ID

ERM-P024

PROPERTY_NAME

Closed-loop test–analyse–fix and FRACAS

HISTORICAL_ORIGIN

Post-war failure reporting, Duane growth, military FRACAS and formal growth-management handbooks.

LINEAGE_CLASS

  1. RELIABILITY_GROWTH_LINEAGE

  2. FAILURE_ANALYSIS_LINEAGE

  3. MAINTAINABILITY_LINEAGE

  4. HYBRID

RELATIONSHIP_TO_OTHER_TRADITIONS

  1. RELIABILITY_ENGINEERING_NATIVE

  2. MAINTAINABILITY_NATIVE

  3. HYBRID_RESOLUTION

ORIGINAL_FORM

Record each failure, analyse cause, implement corrective action and track MTBF growth.

PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED

Testing and field incidents generate no reliability improvement when failures are merely logged, classified or closed administratively.

REQUIRED_FUNCTION_OR_MISSION

Remove or bound recurrent failure mechanisms in the current design/support configuration and verify the changed mission outcome.

FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT

Selective reporting; “no trouble found”; delayed fixes; fix not configuration-bound; no retest; curve fit substitutes for causal closure.

MECHANISM

Unique failure identity; exposure/context capture; causal analysis; prioritised design/process correction; independent retest; recurrence monitoring; fleet/configuration propagation.

TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT

Development test failures, repeated field events, unexplained downtime, reliability-growth commitments or high-cost maintenance families.

NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH

For isolated low-consequence failures, make the obvious reversible correction and verify function without standing up a heavy FRACAS board.

DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS

Data quality, ownership, change authority, configuration traceability, representative retest and recurrence horizon.

OPERATIONAL_PROFILE_PRECONDITIONS

A declared and sufficiently representative operating profile, environment, duty cycle, mission phase and maintenance state.

FAILURE_MODEL_PRECONDITIONS

A failure model appropriate to the estimand; distinguish non-repairable life, recurrent repairable events, demand failures, software activation and service outcomes.

REDUNDANCY_OR_COMMON_CAUSE_PRECONDITIONS

No redundancy premise is needed; if redundancy appears in the implementation, common-cause and switchover assumptions must be analysed separately.

DIAGNOSTIC_COVERAGE_PRECONDITIONS

Observable functional outcomes sufficient to determine whether the claimed property actually holds.

RECOVERY_OR_REPAIR_PRECONDITIONS

No recovery premise is inherent; any claim that depends on restoration must invoke explicit recovery postconditions.

MAINTAINABILITY_PRECONDITIONS

Access, modularity, safe isolation, procedures, test equipment, spares, tools, skills, logistics, configuration data and time under actual field conditions.

EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF

Converts failure experience into verified design/support improvement rather than incident accumulation.

DECISION_OR_CONSUMER

Design authority, reliability manager, maintainer, fleet/service owner and acceptance authority.

KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES

Ticket closure gaming; duplicate fragmentation; symptom fixes; selective test continuation; unimplemented fleet fixes; no recurrence denominator.

IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS

FRACAS can become labour-intensive bureaucracy and reliability-growth models can reward test selection or delayed fixes.

HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED

Failure logs evolved toward configuration-bound causal corrective action, delayed-fix treatment, recurrence families and decision-focused closure.

MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM

A proportionate closed loop in which every material failure is tied to exposure, cause confidence, corrective change, verification and recurrence outcome.

CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY

The database or review board is not the property; verified reduction of the causal failure mechanism is.

NEXT_LEVEL_EFFECT

Effect on the enclosing function, dependency path or maintenance process.

END_EFFECT

Selective reporting; “no trouble found”; delayed fixes; fix not configuration-bound; no retest; curve fit substitutes for causal closure.

PROPAGATION_PATH

Functional, physical, state, timing, control, resource or organisational coupling.

CONTAINMENT_BOUNDARY

DEPENDENCIES

COMMON_CAUSE_OR_SHARED_COUPLING

LATENT_CONDITION

ERM-P025 — Corrective-action effectiveness and recurrence control
PROPERTY_ID

ERM-P025

PROPERTY_NAME

Corrective-action effectiveness and recurrence control

HISTORICAL_ORIGIN

FRACAS corrective-action verification, root-cause analysis and field reliability feedback.

LINEAGE_CLASS

  1. RELIABILITY_GROWTH_LINEAGE

  2. FAILURE_ANALYSIS_LINEAGE

  3. CONVERGENT_ENGINEERING

RELATIONSHIP_TO_OTHER_TRADITIONS

  1. RELIABILITY_ENGINEERING_NATIVE

  2. CONVERGENT_PROPERTY

ORIGINAL_FORM

Close a problem report after the corrective action passes its verification test.

PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED

A local patch or ticket closure suppresses the symptom while the causal family, sibling configurations or enabling conditions persist.

REQUIRED_FUNCTION_OR_MISSION

The relevant failure family no longer occurs, or its frequency/consequence is demonstrably reduced, across the intended population and configuration.

FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT

Fix addresses only one manifestation; test cannot reproduce cause; exposure disappears; sibling systems unpatched; workaround becomes permanent.

MECHANISM

Define recurrence family and causal claim; propagate change; reproduce or challenge mechanism; monitor normalised recurrence and unintended effects.

TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT

Repeated incidents, fleet-wide defect, common supplier/configuration, high-severity failure or claimed reliability growth.

NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH

For a simple deterministic defect, a regression/functional test plus configuration deployment proof may suffice.

DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS

Failure taxonomy, causal evidence, population/configuration inventory, exposure denominator, change deployment and follow-up period.

OPERATIONAL_PROFILE_PRECONDITIONS

A declared and sufficiently representative operating profile, environment, duty cycle, mission phase and maintenance state.

FAILURE_MODEL_PRECONDITIONS

A failure model appropriate to the estimand; distinguish non-repairable life, recurrent repairable events, demand failures, software activation and service outcomes.

REDUNDANCY_OR_COMMON_CAUSE_PRECONDITIONS

No redundancy premise is needed; if redundancy appears in the implementation, common-cause and switchover assumptions must be analysed separately.

DIAGNOSTIC_COVERAGE_PRECONDITIONS

Observable functional outcomes sufficient to determine whether the claimed property actually holds.

RECOVERY_OR_REPAIR_PRECONDITIONS

No recovery premise is inherent; any claim that depends on restoration must invoke explicit recovery postconditions.

MAINTAINABILITY_PRECONDITIONS

Access, modularity, safe isolation, procedures, test equipment, spares, tools, skills, logistics, configuration data and time under actual field conditions.

EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF

Distinguishes real engineering learning from administrative closure and incident-count decline.

DECISION_OR_CONSUMER

Design owner, service/fleet owner, acceptance authority and users exposed to recurrence.

KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES

Reclassification; denominator change; silent suppression; partial rollout; collateral regression; long-latency recurrence.

IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS

Causal certainty may be unattainable for rare complex failures; monitoring can delay closure indefinitely.

HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED

Single-event root cause evolved toward failure-family, system-condition and recurrence-effectiveness tracking.

MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM

A bounded causal claim supported by mechanism-targeted tests, deployment evidence and exposure-normalised recurrence surveillance.

CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY

A “root cause” field or closed status is not the property; changed recurrence risk is.

Provider postmortems commonly list safeguards and architectural changes, but only subsequent representative evidence can establish recurrence reduction.

Absence of recurrence is weak when exposure is small or configuration/profile changed.

How can causal effectiveness be estimated for singular catastrophic events without pretending statistical replication?

ERM-P026 — Reliability demonstration with explicit confidence, population and assumptions
PROPERTY_ID

ERM-P026

PROPERTY_NAME

Reliability demonstration with explicit confidence, population and assumptions

HISTORICAL_ORIGIN

Life tests, binomial/Poisson demonstration plans, military qualification and statistical acceptance testing.

LINEAGE_CLASS

  1. LIFE_DATA_AND_STATISTICAL_RELIABILITY_LINEAGE

  2. HARDWARE_RELIABILITY_LINEAGE

  3. HYBRID

RELATIONSHIP_TO_OTHER_TRADITIONS

  1. STATISTICAL_RELIABILITY_ANCESTRY

  2. RELIABILITY_ENGINEERING_NATIVE

  3. HYBRID_RESOLUTION

ORIGINAL_FORM

Run a prescribed number of units/hours with an allowed failure count to accept an MTBF or reliability target.

PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED

A test pass is overclaimed without stating confidence, sample/population, censoring, model, failure scoring or representative exposure.

REQUIRED_FUNCTION_OR_MISSION

Bound the probability of the ERM-P001 functional failure for a defined population/profile/configuration at a declared uncertainty level.

FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT

Zero failures treated as certainty; dependent trials; post-hoc stopping; excluded failures; nonrepresentative loads; mean used for a tail requirement.

MECHANISM

Predeclare estimand, population, sample design, exposure, scoring, censoring, model and confidence; report power and what remains untested.

TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT

Contractual acceptance, launch/release, supplier qualification, life claim, safety/mission decision or expensive design trade-off.

NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH

Use deterministic functional verification or a coarse bound when probability precision cannot change the decision.

DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS

Stable configuration, representative units/profile, independent or modelled dependence, calibrated instruments and failure adjudication.

OPERATIONAL_PROFILE_PRECONDITIONS

A declared and sufficiently representative operating profile, environment, duty cycle, mission phase and maintenance state.

FAILURE_MODEL_PRECONDITIONS

A failure model appropriate to the estimand; distinguish non-repairable life, recurrent repairable events, demand failures, software activation and service outcomes.

REDUNDANCY_OR_COMMON_CAUSE_PRECONDITIONS

No redundancy premise is needed; if redundancy appears in the implementation, common-cause and switchover assumptions must be analysed separately.

DIAGNOSTIC_COVERAGE_PRECONDITIONS

Observable functional outcomes sufficient to determine whether the claimed property actually holds.

RECOVERY_OR_REPAIR_PRECONDITIONS

No recovery premise is inherent; any claim that depends on restoration must invoke explicit recovery postconditions.

MAINTAINABILITY_PRECONDITIONS

The design and support system must not make verification or corrective action infeasible.

EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF

Makes evidential strength and residual uncertainty visible and prevents “test passed” from becoming an unlimited reliability claim.

DECISION_OR_CONSUMER

Acceptance authority, mission owner, designer, regulator/procurer and service owner.

KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES

Low power; sample substitution; censoring bias; correlated units; success-run overclaim; arbitrary confidence; changing configuration.

IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS

Classical confidence may be misread; large samples can be unaffordable; model-based claims remain assumption-sensitive.

HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED

Pass/fail qualification evolved toward estimand-specific, confidence- and uncertainty-explicit demonstrations plus field updating.

MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM

A decision-calibrated evidence statement: claim, denominator, configuration/profile, model/assumptions, uncertainty, coverage and expiry.

CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY

A qualification certificate is not the property; the bounded evidence claim is.

CURRENT_STATUS

  4. SRC-048

EMPIRICAL_OR_FIELD_EVIDENCE

Incident evidence shows qualification cannot cover every operational combination; field monitoring and configuration binding remain necessary (SRC-058–SRC-062).

CONTRARY_EVIDENCE

Under stable assumptions and representative sampling, demonstration tests provide strong and interpretable evidence.

OPEN_QUESTIONS

How should demonstration confidence be combined with mechanism knowledge and post-deployment evidence without double-counting?
